# Frontend context map

## Audit markers, where exports are saved, the import review's outside files, kept style rules for a page's script, the keys of the toolbar, the property search as a list, and menus during a trial · v0.35.0 (2026-11-27)

The fourth of five releases (0.32.0 to 0.36.0) that answer the follow-up on issue #36. No engine or model change and no new engine command; the review JSON and the `formweb-download` event detail grow by additive fields only. `backend/HASHOUT_LOG.md`, same version, has the service side of the import review. New: `features/audit-markers.ts`, `styles/audit-markers.css`, `features/export-folder.ts`, `styles/export-folder.css`. Changed: `ui/downloads.ts`, `features/studio.ts`, `vector/panel.ts`, `vector/exportscreens.ts`, `features/library/` (`site-import.ts`, `bring.ts`, `importer.ts`, `types.ts`), `styles/library.css`, `features/convert/` (`index.ts`, `measure.ts`, `rules.ts`), `styles/convert.css`, `commands/catalogue.ts` (`view.audit-markers`, and the `inspector.search` description), `features/inspector/search.ts` and `styles/inspector-search.css` (the property search is a list only; see the v0.31.0 entry, rewritten for this release), `features/try/index.ts` (`PASS`, and parking a trial while the menus are used), `features/try/choice-list.ts` (two guards), and in `main.ts` `closeDialog()`, the `installInspectorSearch` call without `select`, the markers' install call and switch row, `auditMarkers.sync(next)` in `update()`, two lines in `auditSite()`, the `view.audit-markers` command, the Preferences row, `useNotice(notice)`, `resetExportFolder()` in the Reset handler, the cancel paths of `downloadProject()`, `exportSite()` and `downloadQr()`, and `data-own-keys` on `.toolbar` and `.fw-title-tools`. The shell's part (`src-tauri/`) is the last paragraphs of this entry.

Audit markers (`features/audit-markers.ts`, `installAuditMarkers(host)` once from `main.ts`, right after `auditList`; it returns `{ row, enabled, set, sync, fresh }`). The host gives the canvas frame, the stage, `#overlay-root`, the dialog scrim, `loaded()`, `page()`, `editing()` (not Live preview, Preview as published, a demo preview, the feature studio or Home), `busy()` (`tryKit.busy()`), `pick(id, message, again)`, `rescue()` and `status`. The switch is stored only in `localStorage['formweb.audit-markers']` with try/catch (`'1'` is on, anything else or nothing is off); Reset All Preferences already removes every `formweb.` key, so its handler needed no line. The kept answer is `{ site, revision, issues }` for the whole site, at most 5,000 issues (`MAX_ISSUES`), stamped with the site id and revision it was asked for; `valid()` compares those with the current state. Every `update()` calls `sync(next)`, which filters the kept answer by the open page (a depth-first walk from the page root, so dots are in tree order) and by the nodes that still exist, so a page switch shows the new page's dots at once and an element removed by another writer loses its dot before the next audit. The one timer (`want()`, `ask()`): a change sets `due`; the request starts at least 150 ms after the change (`SETTLE_MS`), at least 600 ms after the last start (`GAP_MS`), and never while a request is out (`out`); a `due` set meanwhile asks again after the answer. An answer is thrown away, and asked again, when the site, the revision or the page moved while it was out, so a late answer never paints dots of an older page or revision. The Audit dialog's own request hands its answer over with `fresh(answer, site, revision)`, where `auditSite()` reads `state()` before the request: before the review it was stamped with the revision current on arrival, so a change made while it was out read as audited. A failed request sets `failed` and `failedAt` (`site:revision`), hides the dots and says "The audit could not be run, so the audit markers are hidden." once per run of failures; `sync()` asks again only when the revision differs from `failedAt` (before the review, every selection click sent another failing request), and the switch, a successful answer or a fresh dialog answer clear it. With the switch off `want()` returns at once, so nothing is asked beyond what the editor asked before.

Placing: `place()` runs in at most one animation frame (`poke()`), and only when something asks: capture listeners on the window for pointer, key, click, `focusin`, `input`, `change`, `scroll` and `transitionend` (a `pointermove` only with a button held), the window's `resize`, ResizeObservers on the stage, the frame and the canvas `<html>`, MutationObservers on the scrim's `hidden`, the body's `class` and the canvas root's `data-fw-dragging` and `data-fw-holding`, capture pointer, `dblclick`, `focusout`, `keyup`, `scroll` and `load` listeners in the canvas document, and `paint()` from `sync()`. Idle with markers on, no frame is asked for. `place()` reads every element box first (through `FrameMap` from `features/canvas-drag.ts`, `still().toParent`, and its `visible()` part of the stage) and writes the transforms after, so 200 dots never force a layout each; the 20 px dot (`--space-5`) is centred on the element's top right corner, converted to window coordinates and rounded (left = round(x) - 10, top = round(y) - 10), so half of it lies outside the element and it does not cover the end of a button's text; it is shown only when that corner lies inside the visible canvas area (the frame's rectangle intersected with the stage's, edges inclusive with 0.5 px of tolerance) and the whole dot inside the stage's rectangle, otherwise hidden, never clamped; its `transform` is written only when its spot changed (`placed`). Resize-driven placement is synchronous on purpose: inside the ResizeObserver callbacks (stage, frame, the canvas `<html>`) and on the window's `resize`, because a ResizeObserver callback runs after layout and before paint, and deferring to `requestAnimationFrame` painted one stale frame per resize; every other trigger still asks for one frame. A click on a dot whose element is no longer in the window's state drops the dot and says nothing; when the click came first and the outside removal lands after it in the same task, the next `update()` within 5 s clears the status line and the notice through `host.unsay(line)`, only if the status line still shows the "Selected ..." line that click produced (`pick` returns that line, '' when it did nothing). Dots are pooled `button.fw-audit-dot` nodes in one `div.fw-audit-marks` in `#overlay-root`, `position: fixed` at `--z-raised`, updated with `keyed()` by node id and capped at 200 (`CAP`); the dialog's note says "Markers are drawn on the first {0} of {1} elements with issues on this page." past the cap and counts the issues with no element. Each dot carries `data-own-keys`, `data-severity` (the worst of its issues: `info` a blue circle, `warning` a yellow diamond, `error` a red square, drawn by `::before`, so shape and color both tell it; the engine sends only warning and info today), its count as text, an `aria-label` through `labelAttr` ("1 issue on {0}", "{0} issues on {1}") and its sentences in `title`. A click (Enter and Space are the button's own) calls `host.pick`, which in `main.ts` opens the element's page, selects it, reveals it on the canvas, scrolls its Layers row and shows "Selected {0}: {1}" in the status line and the notice with Show again, as the dialog's Select does; with two or more issues one popover (`div.fw-audit-pop`, `role="group"`, "Issues on {0}", `--z-popover`) lists the sentences with `keyed()`, placed under the dot and right-aligned, above it when there is no room, 8 px inside the window, following the dot on every `place()`. It closes on Escape on its dot, a press outside it and the dots, focus moving anywhere but its own dot, and when its dot hides or keeps fewer than two issues. Pointing only shows the native tooltip.

When dots hide (`blocked()`): the switch off, no answer yet, a failed request, the answer for another site, `editing()` false, `busy()`, the scrim shown, `data-fw-dragging` or `data-fw-holding` on the canvas root, `body.fw-canvas-dragging`, or a `[contenteditable="true"]` in the canvas. The element lookup (`resolve(id)`, cached while connected) takes the first `[data-fw-id]` that is not a repeated collection's `:list` wrapper (a `display: contents` div with the same id) and has client rects or is itself `display: contents`, else its script poster (`:scope>[data-fw-poster=script]`), so a collection item repeated on the canvas has one dot, on its first copy. Focus: before any hide, and before a focused dot is dropped from the list or scrolled out of view, `rescue()` walks `host.rescue()`, which is `[the active inspector tab button, the toolbar's Audit button]`, and focuses the first that is connected, rendered (client rects; a collapsed right dock leaves the tab without any), enabled and really takes the focus, never the body. Nothing is added to the canvas document for the markers: the module only listens and observes there, the canvas policy is unchanged, and no rule in the frame changes for them. The sentences in the tooltip, the popover and "Selected {0}: {1}" are the engine's English audit messages in every language, as in the dialog's list; they are not keys. `view.audit-markers` (View, checkable, no key, `enabled: ready`) is in the try kit's `PASS` set, so turning markers on or off never ends a trial. Not handled: during a motion preset's playback a dot stays at the element's resting place until the next poke, because the player's state is not visible outside `features/motion/`.

Where exports are saved, the page side (`features/export-folder.ts`): the choice is `localStorage['formweb.export-folder']`, JSON `{ mode: 'downloads' | 'folder' | 'ask', path }`, read with try/catch; a value over 4 KiB, malformed JSON, an unknown mode or folder mode without a path counts as Downloads, and Downloads with no path removes the key. `hand(choice, forget)` sends it to the shell with `invoke('set_export_place', { mode, path, forget })` when the module loads and on every change; the shell answers `{ mode, folder }` and `settle()` makes the page follow it (when the shell refuses folder mode the page stores Downloads, otherwise the shown path mirrors the shell's record), because the shell, not the page, owns the folder. `exportFolderRow()` is built once: in a browser one sentence ("In a browser, the browser decides where downloads go."); in the desktop app a `radiogroup` with `data-own-keys`, three radios drawn with the checkbox tokens, and under the second a line of a fixed height (`min-height: var(--control-height)`) with Choose folder... (`invoke('choose_export_folder')`, no argument; `aria-busy` while the dialog is open) and the path as text in `span.fw-export-path` with `data-no-i18n`, cut with an ellipsis and whole in its `title`, or "No folder chosen yet.", so a path appearing moves nothing. Picking "A folder you choose" with no folder opens the dialog, and Cancel puts the radio back. `resetExportFolder()` removes the key and sends `forget: true`. `beforeDownload(name)` asks only in ask mode: `invoke('ask_export_place', { name })`, true means go on; a rejected invoke means no download (the first version let the download through without a dialog).

`ui/downloads.ts`: `deliver(file, words, notice, fetch, button?)` now returns `Headers | null`, null when the person cancelled; callers set "Nothing was saved." (`downloadProject()`, `exportSite()`, `downloadQr()` in `main.ts`). In ask mode in the desktop app deliveries run one after another through one promise chain (`turns`). `send()` marks the busy button with `aria-disabled` and `aria-busy`, not `disabled`: a focused button that becomes disabled drops the focus to the page body in the test browser, where Backspace runs `edit.delete`; the command stays refused while busy through `isBusy` in its `enabled()`, and `notice.css` keeps the busy button undimmed with its pulse, as in 0.34.0. The ask comes before the fetch, so the dialog opens before anything is made; the fetch's `started(url)` registers the URL for the shell's `formweb-download` event (`whenSaved`, ten minutes), and a failed fetch after an answer calls `forgetAnswer()`, which hands the choice over again and so drops waiting answers in the shell. The notice reads the event: `reason: 'not-asked'` gives "Nothing was saved."; `ok: false` the could-not-be-saved sentence; a non-empty `unusable` the red "The chosen folder {0} is missing or cannot be written, so {1} was saved to {2}." with Show in folder; a folder the `placed` words with Show in folder; outside Downloads mode "Saving {0}..." shows until the event. `deliverFile(file, fetch)` is `deliver()` with the "Saved {0} to ..." words and the notice handed over once by `useNotice(notice)` in `main.ts`, saying "Nothing was saved." on a cancel; `saveBlob(blob, name, started)` clicks a hidden link for a file made in the page and reports its URL first, as `api.ts` `download()` does. The Website studio's downloads (`features/studio.ts`, `{ downloaded: null }` after a cancel), Export selection as SVG (`vector/panel.ts`) and Export for Screens (`vector/exportscreens.ts`) go through them: Export for Screens stops at the first cancel, says "Exported {0} file." or "Exported {0} files." through `tn`, or "Nothing was saved.", and its Go button uses `aria-disabled` with `run()` returning while it is set (`.fw-v-btn[aria-disabled='true']` is dimmed in `styles/export-folder.css`). Why the shell matches an ask answer by name and not by URL: `api.ts` `download()` makes the blob URL and clicks it in the same synchronous step, so the page cannot tell the shell the URL before the web view asks; running ask-mode deliveries one at a time is what makes the name match exact.

The import review's editor side (`features/library/`): `types.ts` has `SiteOutside` and optional `outside` and `outsideMore` on `SitePage` and `LibResult`, and optional `outsideWarnings` on `SitePage`. `fillPage()` leaves out of the row's notes the warnings whose index is in `outsideWarnings` (the service marks the older outside-script and head-stylesheet sentences that `outside` names in full), so the row says each thing once; nothing matches English text, and a warning the service does not mark (a `data:` script, a refused host, more than 20 files, an `@import` warning) stays on screen. Each review row (`site-import.ts` `row()`, `fillPage()`) has a fourth span, `.fw-lib-site-outside` with `data-no-i18n`, written with `text()`, hidden when the page has no outside files and on skipped rows: "This page loads files from the web: {0}. These files are not brought along.", grouped by kind and host with counts ("2 stylesheets from styles.example.com"), four groups at most, then "1 more file" or "{0} more files" (which includes `outsideMore`), joined by `importer.ts` `joined()` with the "{0} and {1}" and "{0}, {1}" keys. Host names are only ever placeholder values written as text, never links and never loaded. The Bring choice is set exactly twice per pick: `bring.choose('elements')` when the pick starts, while the review is hidden, and once when the review answer arrives, `'code'` when any page has an outside file; nothing else calls `choose()`, so a paint, a language or theme switch or the pages or section toggle never overturns the person's pick. `bring.ts` `explain(why | null)` fills `div.fw-lib-bring-why`, mounted once under the options: both sentences (for Code sections and for Editable elements) sit in one grid cell (`grid-area: 1 / 1`) and the one not picked is `visibility: hidden`, so the box always has the height of the longer one in any language and switching moves nothing; keep that pattern rather than a fixed height. The box has `data-no-i18n`, the radiogroup `aria-describedby` points at the visible line, and `onLanguage(() => this.paint())` rebuilds the composed sentences on a language switch. After an import as elements the result adds `missingHosts()` ("Files from {0} were not brought along, so part of the look or behaviour may be missing.", the first three hosts then "and N more hosts" through `hostList()`), on the pages path and, from the `outside` the import-html answer now carries, on both section paths. The paste path has no review and still starts on Editable elements. Wording: the reasons say what code mode really gives in this wave (the page's own scripts and style rules); the outside head links and scripts are carried in neither mode until Wave 5's consented download.

Kept style rules for a page's script (`features/convert/`): when a conversion keeps a script, `measure.ts` `keepRules()` keeps the page's style rules that the converted elements do not need, for what the script builds after the page loads. "Used" means a selector matched at least one converted element in the hidden measuring frame (`querySelectorAll`, the page as written, no script run); converted elements are the root body, every element that became a node, and everything inside a part kept as code with its look written in. Unused selectors are kept, and a used one too when its subject requires classes that all appear as words of the kept script (`rules.ts` `scriptWords`, `subjectClasses`), because the script may build more of them (the class-word rule; without it built pills lost their padding and radius). In a selector list only the kept selectors stay; custom properties of every rule stay unguarded so kept rules find their `var()` values. Every kept selector is scoped inside the converted root with the existing `scopeSelector` and its subject gets `:where(:not([data-fw-node]))` (`guardSelector`, before any pseudo-element, the one-colon `:before`, `:after`, `:first-line` and `:first-letter` included), which adds no specificity and cannot match an exported element, since every one carries `data-fw-node`. A subject that requires a class is left unguarded on purpose: converted elements carry no class, and a script that gives a converted element a class (an open menu, a pass pill) should get its rule; such a rule, `!important` included, can then restyle that element in Preview and the export, a limit on record. Selectors on a qualified body (`body.dark ...`, `bodyQualified`) are left out, because scoping would drop the condition. At-rules: `@media`, `@supports`, `@container` and `@layer` blocks keep only their kept rules; `@layer` statements keep only names a kept block uses; `@keyframes`, `@font-face` and `@property` stay only when a kept rule names them and never take a name the site already uses (`index.ts` `siteNames()`: the lower-case words of the site's node styles and breakpoints, tokens, `design` and code, without the sections being converted): a clashing keyframes name is renamed `<name>-<root id>` through the CSSOM with `animation-name` rewritten, a clashing font face or registered property is left out and counted ("... were left out because the site already uses their names."); `@import` never stays, and `@page`, `@counter-style`, `@scope` and `@namespace` are left out. Parsing and serialising are the browser's: a constructed `CSSStyleSheet` from the measuring frame's own window, `replaceSync`, `cssRules`, `cssText`; each emitted rule is checked with `insertRule` on a scratch sheet. Do not parse the CSS with regular expressions here, and do not read the frame's applied sheet, whose `url()` values were replaced for measuring; outside `url()` values are kept as written, the same policy as any code section's CSS. Bounds: 3,000 rules and 192 KiB per script (`KEEP_RULES`, `KEEP_BYTES`, under the engine's 256 KiB for a section's CSS), the rest counted in a sentence; matching may take 2,000 ms (`MATCH_MS`), checked before every selector, and past that every rule of the stylesheet is kept unchecked, still scoped and guarded, with its own sentence; the work yields every 40 ms. The 2 s bound covers only matching: a pathological sheet still costs one long style pass in the frame, as in 0.34.0. Where it goes: on a whole conversion the Page script node's `code.css`, for inline-script parts the first Script node, and for a part conversion appended to the remaining code section's CSS (`plan.rest.kept`); it travels in the conversion's own batch, so undo and redo stay two steps. `index.ts` `dropKept()`: the engine checks a section's CSS only by length, so when the batch is refused with "invalid css" or "site exceeds", all kept CSS is taken out, counted in `keptDropped`, and the batch is sent again ("... were left out because the page's code would be too large."), instead of failing the conversion. `rules.ts` `Outcome` gains `kept`, `keptAll`, `keptOver`, `keptClash` and `keptDropped`, and `summary()` the matching `tn()` sentences ("Kept {0} style rules for the content the page's script builds."). With the script not kept nothing changes except the height fix below and the line heights. Line heights (`measure.ts` `lineRatios()`, after the walk, on the root and every converted element whose written `line-height` is in px): the measuring frame reports a computed px value, and a px line height is inherited as px, so a body with `font: 15px/1.5` gave the script's 13 px table cells 22.5 px instead of 19.5 px. A probe child (`fw-probe`, display block, font size twice the element's plus 3 px) is appended in the scriptless frame, read and removed; when its line height follows its own size at the parent's ratio (and is not the parent's px) the ratio is written unitless, otherwise the px value stays, as for authored px or em line heights; `normal` was already written as `normal`. The element's own line box is unchanged. Known gap, not this module's: the export's base `button,input,select,textarea{font:inherit}` gives script-built controls the page font, a few px taller than the browser's own control font.

Filling the canvas: the measuring frame is read with `html{display:none}`, so `100vh` reads as the frame's 900 px and `height: 100%` stays a percentage that the root's new parent cannot resolve, and converted pages collapsed to their content or kept 900 px. `measure.ts` `fillWindow()` reads every written `height`, `min-height` and `max-height` in px or % a second time with the frame 1,300 px tall (`TALL`) and writes the ones that moved in vh again (`inVh`, with `calc(Nvh +/- Mpx)` for a fixed part); a root whose height or min-height was a percentage of a window-high `html` becomes `min-height: Nvh`, and its direct children's percentages become min-heights in vh. It changes only what the converter writes, never a canvas-wide rule, and resizes the frame only when some written height is px or %. It depends on the frame being 900 px tall (`FRAME_H` against `.fw-convert-frame` in `convert.css`): change both together. The canvas frame has a fixed height, so vh cannot feed back.

The convert dialog's focus: `div.fw-convert` is `tabindex="-1"` with `data-own-keys` (`.fw-convert:focus { outline: none; }`); `setBusy(true)` moves the focus to the box before Convert is disabled, and `close()` moves it, when it was inside, to `.inspector-tabs button.active` if rendered, else `[data-cmd="page.audit"]`, before hiding. Before, the disabled Convert button dropped the focus to the body and a Backspace during a conversion removed the selected section. `finish(done, chosen)` selects the conversion's root only when the selection is still what it was when Convert was pressed (or no longer exists). Two existing gaps the builder named and left: a second part conversion of the same section scopes CSS that is already scoped (`[data-fw-node="host"] [data-fw-node="host"] ...`, in `scopedCss` for the remaining code, as in 0.34.0, and now also for rules kept for an earlier part); and a canvas click shortly after a pasted section import bounces the selection back to the imported section after about 0.7 s, also on 0.34.0. Rules for content a script appends outside the converted root, and rules that depend on body classes, do not apply.

Keys: `.toolbar` and `.fw-title-tools` carry `data-own-keys`, so Delete and Backspace after a click on a toolbar button (Audit, Export, a breakpoint button) no longer run `edit.delete` while Mod shortcuts still reach the editor; only Delete and Backspace are plain-key registry shortcuts and Escape for dialogs has its own listener, so no toolbar control loses a key it needs, and its inputs and selects are typing areas anyway. The try kit's `PASS` set (`features/try/index.ts`) gains `view.audit-markers` and `view.hover-outline`. `closeDialog()` in `main.ts`: after `lastFocus?.focus()`, when focus is on nothing, on the body or still inside the scrim (a dialog opened from a menu row, whose `lastFocus` was that hidden row), it goes to the active inspector tab button, else the toolbar's Audit button, so closing such a dialog no longer leaves Delete and Backspace on the page. Still open, older than this release: a menu command started with the mouse and no trial on leaves the focus on the body (`Menubar.close()` in `ui/shell.ts` restores it only when `returnTo` is set, and `returnTo` is set only for a menu opened by keyboard), so Edit > Delete with the mouse followed by Backspace can remove a second element; the fix belongs in `Menubar` (remember the focused element outside `.fw-menu` when a menu opens by pointer too).

Menus during a trial (`features/try/index.ts`, `choice-list.ts`): focus moving into the menu bar or one of its menus parks a trial instead of reverting it, because the person has not chosen anything yet; the command decides. A `PASS` command keeps the trial; `edit.undo` reaches `intercept` with the trial still on, which reverts it and uses that Undo up, so the next Undo really undoes; any other command is reverted first by `intercept`, then runs. A menu closed with Escape, Tab or a click away leaves the trial on when focus comes back to the owner or the bar; focus landing anywhere else goes through the normal `focusin` rule, and a press on the canvas reverts as before. "A menu of the menu bar" is `.fw-menubar`, or a `.fw-menu` while a menu bar button has `aria-expanded="true"` (`MENUS`, `inMenubar()`), because the context menus share the `.fw-menu` class and should still end a trial. Chromium leaves focus on a menu row that has just been hidden and then drops it to the body, so the kit puts focus back itself (`unpark()`, one task after `intercept` and on each watcher frame while parked) to where it came from, else the anchor, else the owner's `inside()` parts, and only when focus is on the body or on a menu node that is no longer rendered, so a dialog or the palette that took focus keeps it. The Undo mark (`aimed`) is now set only from toolbar `[data-cmd]` buttons, which still take focus and revert on press; keep it, since `intercept` there finds no trial. The choice list closes and reverts on its own outside press and `focusin`, so `outside()` and `moved()` in `choice-list.ts` return early while a trial is on and the target is in `.fw-menubar, .fw-menu` (`inMenus`); without that, Edit > Undo by mouse during a list trial reverted the list on the press, the mark was never set, and the Undo removed a real kept change.

The shell (`src-tauri/src/downloads.rs`, the `invoke_handler` list in `main.rs`, `build.rs`, `capabilities/default.json`). The folder is the shell's, not the page's: only `choose_export_folder` (an async command that opens the system folder dialog through `tauri-plugin-dialog`'s `DialogExt`, parented to the main window, starting in the recorded folder or Downloads) can record one, so a script that reaches `invoke` cannot name a folder. The record is kept in memory (`CHOSEN`, read once) and in `export-folder.json` in Tauri's `app_config_dir()` (the first file the shell stores), `{"folder": "..."}`; a missing, malformed, empty, relative or oversized record means none. `write_record()` writes a temporary file in the same folder and renames it over the old one, so a crash leaves the old record or the new one, and writing none removes the file. `set_export_place(mode, path, forget)` answers `{ mode, folder }`: folder mode is taken only when a folder is recorded and the page's path is empty or names it (`place_of`), ask is ask, anything else Downloads; `forget: true` deletes the record (Reset All Preferences); every call drops the waiting dialog answers. `ask_export_place(name)` opens the save dialog with the cleaned name before the page starts its download and keeps the place for 30 minutes (`ANSWER_LIFE`), used once, replaced by the next answer for the same name, at most four kept; false after Cancel. Dialogs are only ever opened in these async commands, never in the download handler, which must not block.

`DownloadEvent::Requested` places every download: the suggested name is hostile, so `clean()` keeps only its last component and returns a name with no separators, drive letters or Windows-refused characters (`<>:"|?*` become `_`), no control or direction characters, no leading dots or spaces, no trailing dots or spaces, at most 200 bytes cut on a character boundary with an extension of up to 16 bytes kept, `download` when nothing is left, and `_` before a reserved device name. Its table, run as a pure function on the real code in a scratch crate: `../../x.zip` gives `x.zip`; `C:\x.zip` `x.zip`; `C:x.zip` `C_x.zip`; `con.zip`, `CON`, `Con .txt`, `nul.tar.gz`, `com1.zip`, `lpt9`, `LPT¹.txt` and `conin$.log` get a leading `_`, `COM10.zip` does not; `a/b.zip` gives `b.zip` and `a\b\c.zip` `c.zip`; `...`, `.`, `..`, `/` and the empty name give `download`; `....zip` gives `zip` and `.hidden` `hidden`; `name. . ` gives `name`; NUL and U+001F are removed; `evil` U+202E `piz.exe` gives `evilpiz.exe`; `café 日本.zip` is kept; a 300-byte name ends at 200 bytes with `.zip` kept. `web_name()` removes the web view's own " (n)" only when the unnumbered name exists in the web view's folder, so a person's "Board (2).svg" stays. `free()` (shared by every system now, and by the Linux `settle()`) gives "base (1).ext", "base (2).ext" up to 9,999 (extension after the first dot, as the web views split), then a millisecond stamp; "taken" is `occupied()` (`symlink_metadata`, so a dangling link counts) or the destination of another download not yet finished, compared without regard to case (`folded`). `usable(folder)`: absolute, an existing directory, and a new file can be created there (`.formweb-write-check-<pid>-<nanos>`, removed at once); it runs on the main thread inside `Requested`, accepted, so a dead network folder can stall the window for the system's timeout. A 200-byte name in a deep Windows folder can pass the 260-character path limit; whether the Windows web view then fails is not known. Downloads mode leaves the web view's destination alone (the web views already number names), except that an unclean suggested name is cleaned in the same folder; a chosen folder that is not usable leaves the web view's place and sets `unusable`; an ask answer whose folder is not usable does the same. In ask mode a download with no live answer is refused: `Requested` returns false (Tauri 2.12.3 then prevents the download), the shell sends `formweb-download` with `ok: false, reason: "not-asked"`, and remembers the request so a later `Finished` for it sends nothing; "Ask each time" is never broken silently. `Finished` sends `url`, `ok`, `file`, `folder` and the new `unusable`; a download the shell placed itself is never moved by the Linux `settle()`, so a chosen folder is not emptied into `~/Downloads`. `build.rs` `APP_COMMANDS` gains the three command names (without them the build fails with "Permission allow-set-export-place not found"), and `capabilities/default.json` allows `allow-set-export-place`, `allow-choose-export-folder` and `allow-ask-export-place` for the editor's remote origin; no dialog plugin permission is needed, because the page never calls the plugin. A hostile script that reaches `invoke` in the editor's origin can switch between Downloads, ask each time and the folder the person picked, open the two dialogs (which the person must answer) and forget the record; it cannot name a folder, cannot write the record file, and in ask mode cannot download without an answer. Lock order is `REQUESTED`, then `PLACE`, then `ANSWERS`, with no cycle.

What was compiled and run: `cargo check --locked` and `cargo check --locked --tests` of the real crate for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, and of a copy of the crate for `aarch64-apple-darwin` (with `CC_aarch64_apple_darwin=clang`), both without warnings from this code; a Linux check fails only for the missing GTK libraries, so `cargo test` of the shell, the existing `show_in_folder...` test included, did not run here. `clean()`, `unnumbered()`, `web_name()`, `free()`, `occupied()`, `usable()`, `place_of()`, `read_record()` and `write_record()` were copied unchanged into a scratch crate and run. The page side was run in the test browser with a stand-in for `window.__TAURI__` that answers as the shell does. Never run: the desktop app on any system, the real dialogs, whether the web views of Windows, macOS and Linux honour the destination set in `Requested` and send a `Finished` after a refused one, Finder and Explorer selecting the file, the record file in a real app, and `blocking_pick_folder` and `blocking_save_file` from an async command on macOS. `docs/DESKTOP_TESTING.md` step 7 is the owner's list.

## Try a choice before keeping it: the trial layer, the try bar, the editor's own list, values shown while dragging, and motion presets played on the canvas · v0.34.0 (2026-11-27)

The third of five releases (0.32.0 to 0.36.0) that answer the follow-up on issue #36. No engine, model or service change and no new command in the engine: a trial is canvas-only, so the revision, undo, other windows, agents and the export see nothing until Keep. New: `features/try/` (`types.ts`, the contract the lead wrote and nobody else edits; `index.ts`, the kit; `bar.ts`; `segments.ts`; `choice-list.ts`), `features/motion/play.ts`, `styles/try-bar.css`, `styles/try-list.css`, `styles/motion-play.css`. Changed: `ui/canvas.ts` (`setLayer`), `features/inspector/controls/index.ts` (the `trial` option), `features/motion/presets.ts`, `commands/registry.ts` (`intercept`), `commands/keymap.ts`, and in `main.ts` the `installTry({...})` call, one `tryKit.sync()` in `update()`, the Preferences row, `view.try-choices`, `tryKit.busy()` in `installHoverOutline`'s `busy`, `commandRegistry.intercept`, the `trial` option of `installControls`, `breakpoint` and `editing` on the inspector context, and `data-own-keys` on `.inspector-tabs`.

The canvas layers (`ui/canvas.ts`): `Canvas.setLayer(name, frame)` with `name` `'play'` or `'trial'` and `frame` `{ [nodeId]: { [cssProperty]: string | null } }`, or `null` to clear. `restyle()` (the style block that `patch()` had, moved out unchanged) draws in this order, later wins: the stored styles with the matching breakpoints applied widest first, the scene scrubber's motion frame (`setMotionFrame`, unchanged, still a full `render()`), `play`, `trial`. A layer key first deletes every own key with the same `cssName`, so a camel and a kebab spelling never both apply. `null` means no own value at the picked breakpoint: on the base breakpoint (maxWidth 100000, or none picked) nothing is put back, on a narrower one the value of the wider matching breakpoints without the picked one is. `safeStyle` applies to layer values as to stored ones. `setLayer` returns at once when the frame's JSON equals the last one, stores a deep copy, and before the first render only stores it (the next render draws it). Otherwise it collects, per node id whose entry changed, the union of the property names in the old and the new entry, and calls `restyle(node, key, site, id, scope, only)` on every mounted node of that id (every repeated CMS copy; `:list` keys skipped): `only` limits the writes and removals to those properties, while `this.styles` for the key is still rebuilt in full, so a later full render or a clear leaves no stale inline value. It never calls `render()`, never creates, reorders or reparents a node. Measured at 4x CPU on Lowlight (298 canvas nodes), median per call: equal frame 0 ms, one node 1.9 ms, 30 nodes 9.1 ms, a full `render()` 29.1 ms. Keep that split if you touch `restyle()`, or motion playback becomes a whole-page render per frame. Trap: an element that had no `style` attribute keeps an empty `style=""` after a layer clears (`removeProperty` leaves the attribute); identical on screen, but an exact-attribute test sees it.

The kit (`features/try/index.ts`, `installTry(options)` once from `main.ts`, created before `installControls` so the control kit can reach it). Options: `scope()` is `<node>|<breakpoint>|<page>`, `node()`, `exists(id)` (`!!lastState.site.nodes[id]`), `stored(key)` and `inherited(key)` at the picked breakpoint, `editing()` (not Live preview, not Preview as published, no scrim dialog, no demo preview, no feature studio, Home hidden), `setStyle`, `status`, `fields` (`styleFields`) and `segments` (`segmentFields`). One trial at most (`current`). `begin(owner)` retires any trial and returns a `Trial` for `owner.key` on the element and breakpoint on screen; it shows nothing. `put(value, name)` stores the value, redraws the layer, calls `bar.show(owner, name)` and starts the watcher; `''` is drawn as `null`. `draw()` builds one `trial` frame from three sources, in order: live drag values (`lives`, all on one node, `liveNode`), the `landing` set, and `current`. `keep()`: no value yet ends as reverted; a changed scope or node, or design left, ends as dropped; a value equal to the stored one (trimmed) sends nothing and ends quietly (the value is cleared first, so `end('reverted')` prints no "Not kept", and `unsay()` clears the kit's own earlier sentence; before this it printed "Not kept: ..." right after the person pressed Keep); otherwise `pending` is set, focus is moved off the bar, `bar.pending(true)`, and `await options.setStyle(key, value)`. `setStyle` reads `currentNode()` and the breakpoint synchronously, so the one command carries the element and breakpoint of the moment Keep was pressed. Success ends as kept; a rejection ends as refused with the engine's or the editor's sentence as an error ("The change was refused." only when none came). `keep()` never rejects: its body is guarded, and a throw inside ends the trial as dropped.

Why the layer is cleared in the continuation after `accept()` drew: in `api.ts`, `settle()` runs `accept()`, which runs `update()` (`tryKit.sync()`, then `canvas.render()` with the new stored value) synchronously, before the command's promise resolves. The `await` continuation in `keep()` (`end('kept')`, `draw()`, `setLayer('trial', null)`) therefore runs in the same task as that render, and no frame shows the old value between Keep and the answer. The 409 path loads the newest state before it rejects, so a refusal shows the stored value at once. If `command()` ever defers `accept`, the layer must instead be cleared in the next `sync()`.

Every ending goes through `entry.end(how, message)` exactly once: `over` is set, the entry leaves `landing`, the bar hides (focus moved off it first), the layer is redrawn, the status line gets the sentence (refused: the message as an error; a given message, today only "The element was changed elsewhere, so the trial was dropped."; reverted with a value shown: "Not kept: {0} is unchanged." with `[{ t: owner.label }]`, so the field name is translated too; kept: the line is cleared only if it still shows the kit's own last sentence, `said`), and then `owner.ended(how)`, inside try/catch with `console.error`, because `sync()` runs in the middle of `update()` and an owner's throw must never stop the canvas and the inspector from following the new state. Who ends what: Keep and Enter call `trial.keep()`, Revert and Escape `trial.revert()` (the owner or the bar). `sync()` from `update()` (`check(true)`): the element no longer exists, dropped with the sentence; scope changed (selection by any path, breakpoint, page) or design left, dropped silently; this key's stored value at this breakpoint differs from the value at `begin` (`base`), dropped with the sentence; any other outside change leaves the trial drawn over the new state. The watcher (`check(false)`, one `requestAnimationFrame` loop while a trial or a live value exists, stopped when neither does) catches design left without an `update()` (Home, a dialog, the full preview) and an anchor that is no longer rendered (a section folded, the inspector tab changed, the property search filtered the field away), which reverts. A capture `focusin` on the document reverts when focus lands outside `owner.anchor`, `owner.inside()` and the bar (not on the body, not in the command palette `.fw-search-pop`). A capture `pointerdown` on the canvas document, attached once after `loaded()` (the kit reads the Canvas's `frame` and `loaded()` the way `hover-outline.ts` does, since `TryCanvas` declares only `setLayer`), reverts a shown, non-pending trial: a press on the element that is already selected changes no selection and focus moving into the iframe fires no `focusin` here, so before this a double-click into the text edited under the tried value. A wheel is not a pointer press and leaves the trial on. Window blur and a `focusout` with no `relatedTarget` do nothing; for the latter, one macrotask later, if the window still has focus and focus is on the body, the kit puts it back on the element that lost it (if still rendered and enabled) or on the anchor, because a press on panel padding blurs the focused segment and Backspace on the body runs `edit.delete`. `begin()` and `live()` retire the trial in progress; `setEnabled(false)` drops it. `drop()`, `active()` and `TryOptions.inherited` exist and nothing calls them.

Pending Keep and the landing set: a pending entry is never reverted or dropped. `retire()` moves it to `landing`, sets `current` to null and `detached` (the owner's `Trial.over` then reads true, so the owner takes input again), hides the bar and calls `owner.ended('dropped')` once; its value stays drawn on its node until the answer, so the element the command targets never shows the old value. A new `begin()`, `live()`, the switch, `drop()`, and `check()` when the scope changes or design is left while pending all retire. So a selection change during a pending Keep hides the bar at once and frees the field for the new element, and the command still lands on the first element. This differs from contract row 12 (which said the layer is cleared): the kept value stays drawn on the first element until the answer, on purpose. A Keep with no answer after 30 s (`LANDING_MS`) is retired and removed from `landing`, so `busy()` and the hover outlines recover; a late answer still draws and ends it.

The intercept hook (`commands/registry.ts`): `Commands.intercept`, a nullable `(id) => boolean` set in `main.ts` as `commandRegistry.intercept = id => tryKit.intercept(id)`. Both `run()` and `call()` ask it right after the command lookup and BEFORE the enabled check: `edit.undo` is enabled only with history, and on a freshly opened demo Mod+Z returned before the kit was asked, so the trial stayed. `run()` (keys, menus, toolbar, palette) returns true without running when the hook returns true. `call()` (the agent bridge `runFromBridge`, which `window.formweb.run` also uses) calls the hook for its side effect only and always goes on to the enabled check and the command: a programmatic call is never swallowed, because an agent that sends `edit.undo` to take back its own wrong change would otherwise be told it ran while its change stayed, and would report it undone. The rule inside the kit: `edit.copy`, `file.save`, `view.search` and `view.try-choices` (`PASS`) never touch a trial. For any other id, with a trial on and not pending, the trial is reverted first; for `edit.undo` the hook then returns true when that trial had a value on the page, so a person's Undo only puts the page back and nothing earlier is undone. With no trial, or a pending one, nothing is reverted, so Undo after a pending Keep queues behind it in `api.ts` and undoes the Keep (if that Keep is then refused, the queued undo removes the previous, unrelated change). The toolbar Undo trap: a pointer press on the toolbar Undo button or the Edit menu focuses it first, the `focusin` rule reverts the trial, and when the command then runs the kit finds no trial, so the undo removed the person's previous real change too. Now, when `focusin` reverts a shown trial and focus landed on a command surface (`.fw-menubar`, `.fw-menu`, `.fw-search-pop`, `[data-cmd]`), the kit stores the time (`aimed`); the next `intercept('edit.undo')` within 15 s (`AIMED_MS`) is used up. `aimed` is cleared by any intercept, by `begin()`, by a pointer press or typed input outside the menus and the palette, and by any key press except Mod+Z and the modifier keys. `edit.undo` has `repeat: true`, so once a Mod+Z is used up its auto-repeats are used up too until the key is released (`swallowing`, `repeat`), or holding the keys walked into real history.

The bar (`bar.ts`, `mountBar(overlay, actions)`): one `div.fw-try-bar` created once in `#overlay-root` with `data-try-bar`, `data-own-keys`, `role="group"`, `aria-label` "Trial" and `tabindex="-1"`; inside, the text "Trying: {0}" (`aria-live="polite"`), Keep and Revert, each with its key from `formatKeys()` (`data-no-i18n`, as menus show keys). It is shown and hidden, never rebuilt, and takes no layout space, so no field moves. `show(owner, name)` unhides first and writes the text after, in the same task and before placement and paint, and `hide()` empties the text: a live region that changes while hidden and appears with its content is not announced, so the first trial was silent. The name goes as `{ t: name }`, so a language switch during a trial translates it. Two rows: "Trying: {0}" fills the top row, cut with an ellipsis, the full text also in its `title` (through `labelAttr`); Keep and Revert sit whole and right-aligned on the second row. Width rule: the width depends only on the panel that holds the owner, the anchor's nearest scrolling parent, or for an anchor in the overlay (the list popover) the scrolling parent of `owner.inside()` (the inspector): panel width minus 6 px on each side, at most the window width minus 16 px, left edge at the panel's left plus 6 px. It is set when a bar is shown for an owner and again only when the panel's width or left edge changes, never on a name change, so arrow keys never move the bar; at that moment, if the content overflows, `is-tight` hides the key hints (both buttons stay whole; seen in German and Russian at a 900 px window, panel 235 px). The pending ring is centered over the whole Keep button, whose text turns transparent while waiting, so no box changes. Placement (`place()`, before the frame is painted): below the box of the anchor together with `owner.inside()` (6 px gap), above when there is more room there, clamped 8 px inside the window; when the anchor has scrolled out of its scrolling parent, pinned to that parent's visible edge. While shown, passive capture `scroll` and `resize` listeners place it again once per animation frame, and a repeat `show()` for the same owner changes only the text and moves only if the anchor box or the bar's width changed. `pending(true)` moves focus off a button that is about to be disabled (to the bar root; a disabled focused button sends focus to the body), sets `aria-busy`, disables both buttons, and after 300 ms shows the ring over Keep (see the width rule above). Focus fallbacks in `hide()` (`rescue()`, only when focus is in the bar): the anchor if it is focusable, else the owner's tried button, else its pressed one, else its first focusable; only when the owner is not rendered, the active inspector tab button. It never walks the page for "the next focusable", which landed on a Reset link, where Backspace still runs `edit.delete`. Keys: Escape anywhere in the bar reverts, Enter on Keep keeps, Tab on Revert or the root calls `leave(true)`, Shift+Tab on Keep `leave(false)` (back to the owner's anchor).

Segment groups (`segments.ts`, `installSegments`): every group in `segmentFields` (Display, Direction, Align, Justify, Wrap, Text alignment). A capture-phase `click` listener on the group runs before the button's own target-phase listener; when `kit.enabled()` it calls `stopPropagation()` and `preventDefault()`, which is what keeps the 0.33.0 write from running, and starts or moves the trial; with the switch off it returns and the old write runs unchanged. Space is left to the native button click and so goes through the same listener. A click on the stored value tries `''` named "Inherit" (the word the selects use for their empty option; "None" read as hiding the element in Display); a click on the tried value keeps trying it. Arrows step from the tried choice, else the stored one, without wrapping, Home and End go to the ends, and focus follows; an arrow that lands on the stored value with no trial on tries nothing. Enter keeps, Escape reverts, Tab with a trial focuses Keep, Delete and Backspace are swallowed, and while pending every key and click is ignored. The marks are `data-trying` on the tried button and on the group (`"unset"` while `''` is tried); `is-on`, `is-inherited` and `aria-pressed` keep meaning stored and are never touched, so `update()` never has to know about trials. Each group gets `data-own-keys` and `tabindex="-1"` once at install, whatever the switch: the kit rescues focus with `owner.anchor.focus()`, a div is not focusable, and the group's `focus` handler forwards to the tried, else the pressed, else the first button (without it, Shift+Tab from Keep left focus in the bar and a hide after Keep could drop it on the body). Side effect, also with the switch off: Delete and Backspace on a segment no longer run `edit.delete`, and Escape on it no longer reaches the editor. `ended()` clears the marks and moves focus only when the focused button is no longer rendered: to the pressed or first rendered segment, else the active inspector tab.

The choice list (`choice-list.ts`, `installChoiceLists`): every `<select data-style>` in `styleFields` (Align self, Justify self, Object fit, Weight, Capitals, Kerning, Position, Overflow). The select stays in the page, visible, and always shows the stored value; a trial never sets `select.value`, and the module has no `change` listener, so a program that sets the value and fires `change` writes at once as before. What Chromium (158, Linux) does natively on a closed select: Up, Down, Left, Right, Home, End, Page Up, Page Down and a typed letter change the value and fire `change`, and a mouse or touch press opens the browser's popup. With the switch on, `keydown` is prevented for all of these: Space, Enter, the arrows, Page Up, Page Down and Alt+Down open our list and try nothing; Home and End open it and try the first or last row; a letter or digit opens it and acts as type-ahead. `mousedown` (left button) is prevented and opens ours; preventing it alone also stops the popup for touch, because a touch tap still sends a compatibility `mousedown` after `pointerdown`. Do not prevent `pointerdown`: that suppresses the compatibility `mousedown`. One `role="listbox"` popover (`#fw-tlist`, `data-own-keys`, `--z-popover`) serves all eight fields; rows are `keyed()` by `v:<value>`, named through `englishOf(option)` and `label()` (else the raw text with `data-no-i18n`), the tick reads `options.stored(key)`, not `select.value`, the tried row carries `data-trying` and an outline, and pointing at a row only tints the row. It is placed against the select before paint (below, or above when there is more room, at least as wide as the select, `max-height` when the room is short with a 96 px floor, re-measured after setting it because the thin scrollbar widens it) and follows the select every frame and on scroll and resize. The owner is `{ anchor: pop, inside: () => [select], label }`, the label being the English text node of the field label. Exactly one ending: every closing path (`close()`, `lost()`, Escape, a pointer press outside, `focusin` elsewhere) calls `trial.revert()` only while the trial is live and not pending, and `ended()` acts only when its owner is the current one, so a second ending from the kit's `focusin` rule or `sync()` is ignored. Focus: `hide()` moves focus to the select before the popover hides; `ended()` returns focus to the select when it was in the list, the bar, on the select or on the body, and leaves it where the person moved it otherwise; `guard()` checks once after a timer and once after two frames, and focus found on the body or in the hidden popover goes to the select, else the section title, else the active inspector tab. Tab from the list with a trial goes to Keep; a capture `keydown` on the document takes Tab on Revert (or the bar root) and moves focus to the next tabbable after the select, because the kit's `leave(true)` searches after the anchor, which here is the popover at the end of `#overlay-root`, and found nothing. Window blur closes the list (no trial) when focus went into the canvas iframe. The stale focused select: `value()` in `ui/dom.ts` skips a focused control, and after Keep focus is on the select, so an undo left it showing the kept value. While a list select has focus, a per-frame check (`hold`, `follow`) sets `select.value` to the stored value, without `change`, only when the stored value itself changed, the list is closed and trying is on, so it never fights a program or a native pick.

Live drags (`features/inspector/controls/index.ts`): `ControlOptions.trial?: { live(key, value), settled(key) }`, wired in `main.ts` to `tryKit.live` and `tryKit.settled`. Only `field.show(value)` puts a value on the page, on the same path that writes the input (gesture alive and in the scope on screen); each field remembers in `Upgraded.live` the scope whose value it put there, so a clear or a settle for an older scope never touches the page and `live(key, null)` is sent only when something is on. The dead or out-of-scope `show()`, the dead-gesture `commit()` and `moved()` (selection or breakpoint change) clear it. `restore()` (Escape, `pointercancel`, a cut-off gesture, the `later()` restore, a same-value release, a refusal) shows the newest value still in flight for that scope when a live value was on, otherwise clears it, so an Escaped drag after a commit that is still unanswered shows that commit, not the older stored value. `settled(key)` is sent from `settle()`, reached from `track()`'s `finally` when the last commit of the scope is answered and no live gesture is open in it; since `accept()` has already drawn the new state by then, the page never steps back between release and answer, and a refusal shows the stored value at once. In the kit a live value retires any trial, all live values sit on one node, and a scope change or leaving design clears them. Typing in a text box is never live. The switch does not turn live drags off: a drag never asks to be kept. Limit: a selection or breakpoint change clears the live layer even while a commit is pending, so the old element can show its older stored value until that answer lands. Trap for a later control: anything passed to `field.show()` goes to the page, so never call it from a pointer that only rests somewhere or with unfinished text. With the brand restriction turned on by another writer during a drag in the color square, the picker now cancels the drag (`color-picker.ts`, `refresh()`), or the next move was measured against the hidden square and drew black on the page.

Motion playback (`features/motion/play.ts`, `presets.ts`): `createPlayer(canvas, live)` returns `play(id, kind, entry, children)`, `stop()` and `playing()`. A preset click sends the same command as before (`ctx.set`, `ctx.remove`, `nodePatch` for the classic effects); after its answer, one frame plus one task later (so the editor's own redraw does not eat the first frames) and only if the revision has not moved, the preset plays once through `canvas.setLayer('play', ...)`, timed by `performance.now()`. Tracks: one per element and animated property; a reveal (`from` keys in `MOTION_SAFE`) runs from `from` to the end value, which is the element's own inline value on the canvas (the stored styles at the picked breakpoint), else its computed value; when that is `none`, the frames in between run toward a neutral value built from `from` (0 for translate and rotate, 1 for scale and opacity, `blur(0px)` and the like for filter) and the last frame writes the real end value, so clearing the layer changes no pixel. Easing names match the site runtime (keywords, `cubic-bezier()`, `spring()` through `interpolate.ts` `easing()`, anything else ease-out); delay and stagger are honoured and `children` uses the node's children. Loops (spin and float only) are a short preview, not the visitor's loop: one cycle squeezed into min(2 s, the cycle), spin a full turn when the amount is a multiple of 360 and there and back otherwise, float up and down with ease-in-out, from the stored rotate or translate. Bounds against hostile entries (imports and older files can carry what the engine now refuses): duration, delay and stagger clamped to 0..10000 ms with non-finite values falling back to 600 and 0, a 20 s cap on the whole playback and on each track's start, at most 100 staggered children, no parsing of any `from`, end, easing or inline value over 200 characters (a long `from` property is skipped, a long end value jumps from `from` to the end), loop amount clamped to plus or minus 100000 and the period to 100..120000 ms, and every number pattern in the one-way form `\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+`. `presets.ts` stops playback in `update()` on any revision change (undo, outside edits, removal; stricter than asked, an unrelated outside edit stops it too), another element, another breakpoint, and `live()` false (reduced motion, design left through `ctx.editing`, the canvas hidden or inert); a preset click and Play again stop it first. Leaving the Motion tab for Design does not stop it. Play again (one row under all the presets, mounted once with a fixed size: the button and `p.fw-play-note`) is disabled when nothing playable is stored or reduced motion is asked for, and moves focus to the first preset before it disables itself. The note says "This effect plays in Preview and on the published site." only when the element has motion (text, counter, pointer, a marquee loop, a classic effect) and nothing the canvas can play, and Play again is then disabled; when something is playable the note is empty and a click on a Preview-only preset plays nothing and says nothing (the first version also showed the note beside a playable effect, so the line contradicted an enabled Play again), and "Your system asks for reduced motion, so nothing is played here." under reduced motion. Two Chromium traps: reading `MediaQueryList.matches` every frame updates its cached value, so its `change` event never comes, and `still()` notices the change itself and refreshes the row; and `ui/help-fit.ts` writes an inline `min-block-size` on every `p.micro` in the inspector to reserve room for translations, so the note is a plain `p.fw-play-note` with a fixed three-line height and a clamp, or the row grew when the note appeared. Fixed, older than this release: `matches()` compared `from` with `JSON.stringify`, and the engine stores `from` with its keys sorted, so Blur in (`{opacity, filter}`) never showed as on; it now compares with sorted keys.

Keys (`commands/keymap.ts`): `select` is a typing area, so no shortcut ran from a focused Design list field and Ctrl/Cmd+Z after a Keep (focus is then on the select) did nothing. The router now lets `edit.undo` and `edit.redo` through on Mod from `select[data-style]`, and only those (a first version let every Mod shortcut through, so Mod+X cut and Mod+D duplicated the element from the Weight list). `.inspector-tabs` and every section title made by `section()` in `main.ts` (`role="button"`, `tabindex` 0; Enter and Space still fold through its own keydown) carry `data-own-keys`, so Delete and Backspace on a tab button or a section title no longer remove the selected element (both were 0.33.0 behaviour; a press on a title focuses it); it is also where the bar's and the segments' focus fallback lands. A click on a field's Reset (`resetButton()` in `main.ts`) first moves focus to the field's input or select, or its stored (`is-on`) or first segment, then writes: the Reset link hides once the value is gone, and focus on a hidden button falls to the body, where Backspace ran `edit.delete`. The bar, the list root and every segment group carry `data-own-keys`; no `data-no-keys` was added.

The switch: Preferences > Interface > Design fields, "Try choices on the page before keeping them", and `view.try-choices` (View, checkable, no key, status "A clicked choice now shows on the page until you press Keep." or "Clicked choices are now written at once."), both calling `kit.setEnabled`. Stored only in `localStorage['formweb.try-choices']` with try/catch: `'0'` is off, anything else or nothing is on; the `formweb.` prefix lets Reset All Preferences clear it. Owners check `kit.enabled()` on every event, so turning it off needs no reinstall: segments fall back to the 0.33.0 write, the lists drop `aria-haspopup` and the select opens the browser's popup, and a trial in progress is dropped (or retired when pending). Hover outlines (0.32.0) are off while `tryKit.busy()` (a trial with a value shown, a live value, or a landing Keep); `busy()` is read only when the pointer moves, so an outline already on screen when a keyboard trial starts stays until the next move.

Limits on record: segment groups or style selects that a plugin adds after `installTry()` runs are not owned and write at once. While a trial waits, the window never goes idle (the kit's and the list's per-frame loops). Not run anywhere: touch and pen beyond the test browser's touch emulation, a screen reader, other browser engines, the desktop web views.

## Controls beside the Design fields: sliders, a color picker with gradients, a grid chooser and the raw CSS switch · v0.33.0 (2026-11-27)

The second of five releases (0.32.0 to 0.36.0) that answer the follow-up on issue #36. No engine, model or service change: style values stay CSS strings, so an older engine opens the same `.fweb` file. Everything below lives in `features/inspector/controls/`, its stylesheets `styles/controls.css` and `styles/control-{slider,color,gradient,grid}.css`, small wiring in `main.ts`, one rule in `commands/keymap.ts`, and the stage head (`styles/stage-head.css`, `styles/shell.css`, `features/crumbs.ts`).

The contract (`controls/types.ts`): a control never owns a value. The text input that `addStyle()` makes stays visible and is the one stored value; tokens, `calc()`, anything typed, agents and `[data-style]` tests keep working, and removing a control cannot lose data. A control gets a `StyleField` with three slots, `lead` (inline before the input), `trail` (inline after it) and `below` (a row under it), mounts its elements into them once when it is created and afterwards only changes attributes, classes, text and `hidden`; popovers are created once in `#overlay-root` and shown or hidden. `sync(value, inherited)` is called after every `update()` and on every `input` and `change` of the input, with the input's text rather than the stored value (so a typed, uncommitted value moves the control too), and returns whether the control can show that value; on `false` the kit sets the field's `data-ctl` to `off`, which hides and inerts the slots but keeps their boxes, so a field never changes height. A control must never throw from `sync` or rewrite what was typed. While a gesture runs it calls `field.show(value)` (the input shows the pending value, nothing is sent), on release `field.commit(value)` exactly once, on Escape or a cancelled drag `field.restore()`; one gesture is one `node.update` and one undo step. `commit()` resolves when the engine answers and rejects with the engine's or the editor's sentence; the kit has then already put the input back, written the sentence to the status line and resynced the control, so a control shows no error of its own.

The kit (`controls/index.ts`): `registerControl(keys, factory)` throws on a second registration of a key; `installControls()` calls the four modules' `register()` once and, for every registered key whose field is a text input, moves the existing input into `span.fw-ctl-row` between `lead` and `trail` (`row.append(lead, input, trail)`, so Tab follows what is drawn), adds `below` after the row, sets `label.htmlFor` to the input (`fw-style-<key>`) and an `aria-label`, and adds the raw switch after the label's Reset. Clicks on anything in a slot that is not a native form control get `preventDefault`, because a `<label>` passes clicks on its content to its first labelable descendant. `main.ts` passes `values(key)` (the stored and the inherited value for the picked breakpoint), `scope(key)` (`<node id>|<breakpoint>|<key>`), `setStyle`, and `setStyles`, which builds one `nodePatch` for several properties at the picked breakpoint (used only by the grid chooser, see below, and it skips the brand check, which covers neither `display` nor `grid-template-*`). `StyleField.commit` skips a value equal to the newest committed or in-flight value, otherwise calls `setStyle` directly rather than `run()` so it can reject.

Values in flight (`index.ts`, `track()` and `shown()`): on a slow engine, answers to earlier commits used to arrive after a newer gesture had begun, and since `update()` writes the stored value into the input, a field stepped back through older values. Now `setStyle()` returns `controlKit.track(name, entered, ...)`, and `update()` fills a field with `controlKit.shown(name, stored)`. Per scope (element, breakpoint, property) the kit keeps the values still in flight and the value a live gesture is showing (`held`); `shown()` returns the gesture's value, else the newest in-flight value, else the stored one. When the last answer of a scope arrives and nothing is held, the scope is dropped and, if it is still on screen and the input is not focused, the stored value is put back and the control resynced; a refused value gives way to the one before it. On blur, a text input with nothing in flight shows the stored value again; that restore is skipped while the window itself is not focused (`document.hasFocus()`), because switching to another app can blur the input without a `change`, and the text typed before the switch must be kept. A gesture belongs to its field and remembers the scope its first `show()` was made in. `field.value()` returns the newest value this field has sent that is still unanswered, for the element and breakpoint on screen, and the stored value only when nothing is unanswered: a control opened right after Enter (the picker choosing its tab, the gradient loading its model, the grid chooser marking an entry, the slider stepping) must start from what was typed, not from the older stored value, or the Background picker opened on Solid over a typed gradient and the next arrow key wrote a solid color over it. Trap: a gesture that never calls `commit()` or `restore()` stays open, and since `shown()` prefers an open gesture, every later `update()` wrote its value into the input until the next selection change (the color square skips its commit when a drag ends on the color it started from, so a drag over a field that an agent changed meanwhile left the agent's value hidden). The kit therefore listens once on `window` (capture) for `pointerup`, `pointercancel` and `keyup`; a pointer release marks open gestures `released` (a new `show()` clears it), and one macrotask later `later()` restores and resyncs a gesture that is still alive, in the scope on screen, with nothing of its own in flight, when it was released by a pointer or its held value equals the newest value. `settle()` runs the same check when the last answer of a scope arrives. The macrotask matters: a control's own commit is often queued in a microtask, and `later()` must see it in flight and leave the gesture alone. Known gap: a gesture released just after another writer removed the element, before that change reached the window, still sends one `node.update` for the removed id; the engine refuses it with a revision conflict, the window loads the newest state and the status line says so, and nothing else changes.

`cancel()` (`Control.cancel?()`): when `sync()` finds that the element or the breakpoint changed (`moved()`, comparing `scope('')`), the kit calls `cancel()` on every control. A control must abandon its gesture at once: no commit, no `show()`, close what is open, release pointer capture, put its own display back; the kit clears the shown value itself and keeps the values that gesture showed as a stale set, so a stray `commit()` of one of them is dropped. For a control without `cancel()` the gesture is kept as dead: later `show()` calls write nothing and its next `commit()` is dropped silently. Before this, a color drag or a held slider key cut off by Edit > Select parent left the uncommitted value in the old element's field until a reload, or committed it to the newly selected element. The slider mutes auto-repeat keydowns of a key held across a cancel until it is released, or the still-held key would start a new step on the new element.

Keys (`commands/keymap.ts`): the key router listens in the capture phase and treats inputs and `[data-no-keys]` as typing areas where it runs no shortcut but global ones. A control that is not a text field but uses plain keys itself (arrows, Home, End, Delete, Backspace, Enter, Escape) carries `data-own-keys` instead: with it the router leaves every key without Ctrl/Cmd to the control and still runs Ctrl/Cmd shortcuts (Undo, Redo, Save). The slider, the color picker root (all its tabs, Gradient included) and the grid chooser panel carry it. The first builds put `data-no-keys` on some and nothing on others: with nothing, Delete or Backspace on the picker's square, hue bar, a swatch or No color ran `edit.delete` and removed the selected element; with `data-no-keys`, Ctrl+Z did nothing while focus sat on a slider, which is where focus stays after every drag. Text inputs inside a control need neither. The kit also puts `data-own-keys` on the three slots and on the raw switch, so focus on the swatch button, the grid button, the unit select or the switch never lets Delete reach `edit.delete` either: after Escape closes the picker, focus sits on the swatch, and a person pressing Backspace "to clear the color" removed the element. The field wrapper does not carry it, so Reset and the older inspector buttons (Display segments, the tabs) still run `edit.delete` on Backspace, as before 0.33.0.

The raw CSS switch and the default: each upgraded field's label gets `button.fw-ctl-raw` (the `code` icon, title "Type {0} by hand: ..."). Pressed, the field's state is `raw`: `lead` and `trail` are `display: none`, `below` is `visibility: hidden` and keeps its height, and `Control.setRaw(true)` lets the control close what it has open. Choices are stored only in `localStorage['formweb.design-controls']` as `{"default": "controls" | "css", "fields": {key: mode}}`, with try/catch, and the `formweb.` prefix makes Reset All Preferences clear it. A per-field choice wins over the default; Preferences > Interface > Design fields (`designFieldsSelect` in `main.ts`) sets the default, its Use for all fields calls `clearRaw()` and is disabled while there are no per-field choices (`InstalledKit.own()`), and `view.design-controls` flips the default with a status sentence. `InstalledKit` (ControlKit plus `own`, `clearRaw`, `track`, `shown`) is the kit's own type; `types.ts` is unchanged by it.

Sliders (`slider.ts`, `number.ts`): one factory for opacity, width, height, max-width, gap, padding, margin, border-radius, font-size, line-height, letter-spacing, top and left. Range and step come only from `SCALES` in `number.ts`, one entry per property and unit, and never depend on, re-centre on or get recalculated from the value held; a value outside the range puts the thumb at the end and is never rewritten. Slider keys never step outward past an end; the text input's Arrow Up and Down step freely, limited only by the floor and ceiling CSS allows. The slider is a `div role="slider"`, not a native range, so that Escape can cancel a drag, inherited and unset values get their own look (`data-state` set, inherited or unset) and exactly one commit happens per gesture. Key steps show at once and commit on keyup, or after a 700 ms pause or a blur in case the keyup is lost, so a held key is one undo step. While a commit is in flight the next key step starts from the value just sent (`flying`), not from the thumb. A unitless 0 counts as the default unit; choosing a unit on an empty field commits nothing and is remembered for the next drag or key. Trap: an extra commit queue in the slider called `field.commit()` after the next key's `show()`, so the kit's gesture tracking no longer matched; `api.ts` already sends commands one after another, so commit at once on release. And the "same as stored, skip" test must compare with the value in flight, or Up then Down back to the stored value is skipped and the input jumps.

Colors (`color.ts`, `color-picker.ts`, `color-area.ts`, `color-math.ts`, `picker-api.ts`): `color` and `background` get a swatch in `lead`, painted by `data-look` (empty, inherited, color, gradient). `sync` returns false for `url()`, several layers, a `var()` that resolves to nothing, a gradient on `color` and anything the browser does not take as a color. The picker is one popover created with the first color control and placed before the first painted frame; a ResizeObserver places it again when its size changes (switching to the Gradient tab makes it taller). It works on a small `PickerTarget` (key, label, anchor, value, inherited, show, restore, commit), not on `StyleField`, so a later wave can open it for any input. A free color is always written as lowercase hex, `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa` below full opacity; a site, brand or team swatch writes `var(--name)`, never the color. Hex, `rgb()` and `hsl()` are parsed in `color-math.ts`; named colors, `oklch()` and other color forms are resolved by the browser through a 2D canvas `fillStyle` read-back, with no copied color table. Site tokens whose value uses `var()` or `calc()` are resolved by a hidden element in the overlay that carries the site's tokens as custom properties (`siteColor()`, two sentinel parent colors tell an unresolved value apart), used only when every `var()` name is a site token so the editor's own variables never leak in. `host.colors()` lists brand, then team, then site tokens, de-duplicated by `css`: `token.define` also puts `--brand-*` and `--lib-*` in `site.tokens`, and listing site first made brand colors arrive as site colors, which emptied the restricted picker. Under the brand restriction the tab strip, square and bars are hidden and only brand and team swatches and No color remain. `Picker.cancel(target)` closes in a quiet mode: drags in every tab are cancelled and `show`, `restore` and `commit` (also the copy of the field `api.field()` hands a tab) do nothing. The eyedropper button exists only when `window.EyeDropper` does. A key step in the square that would not change any 8-bit channel repeats its step (up to 100 times) until the written color changes, so one arrow press is never dead. A hue change (pointer or keys) that starts from a color with saturation or brightness under 5 % first sets saturation 70 % and brightness 85 %, because black, white, grey and unset have no hue and a click on the hue bar otherwise did nothing. Focus guard at the picker level: while the picker is open a MutationObserver on the popover (childList and the attributes `hidden`, `inert`, `disabled`, `class`), every `focusout` without a `relatedTarget` and every `refresh()` schedule a check for the next frame; when the window has focus and the focus is on the body, on nothing, or on a node inside the popover that is no longer rendered, hidden, inert or disabled, focus goes to the active tab's button, or to the popover root (`tabindex="-1"`, which carries `data-own-keys`), and the picker stays open. Without it, an undo that hid the focused part dropped focus on the body, where the next Backspace deleted the element. A real focus move outside, a click outside and window blur still close the picker. The swatch list (`.fw-cp-sets`) is the one part of the popover that shrinks and scrolls (`min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto`), everything else is `flex: none`, so No color never scrolls out of view; arrow keys in a swatch row call `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })`.

Picker tabs (`registerPickerTab` from `picker-api.ts`): a tab is `{ id, label, accepts(key), mount(panel, api) }`, and `mount` runs once when the picker exists and returns `{ open(value): boolean, close() }`. On every open, each accepting tab's `open(value)` is asked in order, and the first to return true is shown, otherwise Solid; a manual switch calls `open(value)` and ignores the result; `close()` runs when the tab is left or the picker closes. `PickerApi` gives a tab `field()`, `colorArea(options)` (a reusable square, hue and opacity block whose `cancel()` returns whether it was dragging), `colors()`, `resolve(color)`, `show(value)` and `commit(value)`. Escape is handled by one of two, never both: the tab's own keydown runs first and acts only when one of its areas cancelled a drag; otherwise the picker closes. Every area a tab creates is recorded, so the picker's Escape and `close()` cancel them all and a release after closing cannot commit.

Gradients (`gradient.ts`, `gradient-model.ts`): the tab parses the stored string into a small model and writes plain CSS. `parseGradient` accepts one `linear-gradient` (angle in deg, or `to <side or corner>`) or `radial-gradient` (circle or ellipse, optional extent, optional `at <position>`) with two or more stops, each any color (token with or without fallback, nested parentheses included) plus an optional percentage from 0 to 100. It returns null for repeating and conic gradients, several layers, color hints, `calc()` positions, two-position stops, non-percent positions, angles not in deg, interpolation hints and unbalanced text, and then `open()` returns false and the value is never touched. The number patterns in `number.ts` (`NUMBER`), `color-math.ts` (`NUMBER`, `gradientColors`) and `gradient-model.ts` (the percent and channel patterns) are written so that the digits can be matched in only one way (`\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+`, not `\d+\.?\d*|\.\d+`): the earlier form let a stored value with a long run of numbers backtrack for seconds, or freeze the window, when the picker opened, and those parsers run on values from imports and agents on every refresh. The stop chips are painted with `background-color` (through `--fw-grad-stop`), never the `background` shorthand, so stored text can never bring in an image. Missing positions are filled in and out-of-order ones clamped as CSS does, so `serializeGradient` can write the stops sorted with every position and still mean the same (checked pixel for pixel in the test browser). `open()` returns true only for a gradient it parsed; for a solid or empty value it prepares a starter (the field's color to a lighter or darker shade, or `color-mix(in srgb, <token> 55%, white)` for a token) and returns false, because the picker shows the first tab that says true; Use gradient commits it.
