Handles are `keyed()` in creation order; ids are reused by rank when an undo or an outside change reloads the model, so the handles keep their nodes and the selected stop keeps its rank. Two traps: the picker closes on any focusout that leaves the popover, so focus must move off a handle or button before it is removed or disabled; and `keyed()` moving a surviving focused handle with `insertBefore` blurred it, focus fell to the body and the next Backspace deleted the hero element, which is why `render()` removes the handles of deleted stops before calling `keyed()`. The tab keeps the strings of its own commits in flight and ignores echoes of them in `open()`, released as each commit settles, so an older state landing late never reloads an older gradient and an undo is never mistaken for one. When `open()` reloads from a value that is no longer a gradient (an undo back to a plain color with a handle focused), `leave()` first moves focus to the picker root, and when handles are removed `rescue()` moves it to a surviving handle or the Linear or Radial button; before this, focus fell to the body and the next Backspace deleted the section with its whole subtree. The picker's own focus guard is the backstop for anything a tab forgets.

Grid (`grid.ts`, `grid-tracks.ts`): `grid-template-columns` and `grid-template-rows` get a button in `lead` drawing the tracks as SVG bars from a pool that only grows, and one `role="dialog"` chooser per axis in the overlay. `parseTracks` draws against a nominal 1000 px box (auto and content keywords 100 px, % against 1000, rem and em 16 px) and understands fr, px, %, rem, em, 0, auto, min-content, max-content, `minmax()`, `repeat(n, ...)` with n from 1 to 100 and one `repeat(auto-fit|auto-fill, ...)`; named lines, subgrid, none, `var()`, `calc()`, `fit-content()`, nested repeat and negative values return null and the kit hides the button. `sameTracks` compares with `repeat()` expanded, so a typed `1fr 1fr 1fr` marks 3 columns; exactly one entry is marked, and the general Any number of entry only when nothing more specific matches. Choosing closes the chooser synchronously and then commits, so nothing is left for `cancel()`. A control sees only its own field, so the chooser reads Display from the inspector's Display segments when it opens (values outside the segments, such as inline-grid, count as not grid). On an element that is not a grid, and when the host has the optional `commitStyles` (the kit wires it to `setStyles`), an entry is only picked (`aria-pressed` plus `.is-picked`), the two-line CSS under Make this element a grid shows what will be written (a fixed two-line block, so it never pushes the entries), and the button sends `display: grid` and the tracks as one command, one undo step, at the picked breakpoint.

Fixed, older than this release: in every style field without a control, clicking the label text clicked Reset (the label's first labelable element, as it had no `for`) and cleared the value; `addStyle()` now sets `control.id = fw-style-<key>` and `wrapper.htmlFor`, and the kit keeps an id that is already set. Segment fields were never affected, their wrapper is a div.

The path above the canvas (`styles/stage-head.css`, `styles/shell.css`, `features/crumbs.ts`): it was hidden below a 400 px head (a container rule hid `.fw-crumbs`) and its current crumb was cut on both sides with no ellipsis. The test browser does not draw `text-overflow` on a `<button>`'s own text, and a button centres its content, so each crumb's name now lives in one `span.fw-crumb-name`, created once in the keyed create callback and written by the update callback, inside an inline-flex button aligned to the start. The trail gets a reserve that depends only on the head's width, never on the selection: `clamp(8.5rem, 24cqi, 14rem)`, `max(5.5rem, 28cqi)` at 560 px or less and `max(5.5rem, 40cqi)` at 400 px or less. The rest is shared by flex-shrink weight: the address gives way first (1000, ellipsis, one line), the width note next (`flex: 0 4 23em`, down to 8em, three clamped lines, its box kept while hidden), the page name last. The flex algorithm hands out only a fraction of the overflow when the shrinkable factors sum to less than 1, so never give the page name a factor below 1. With the scene scrubber showing, the note is not shown at 900 px of head or less; at 560 px or less the scrubber's scene select and output go and its range is 44 px; at 400 px or less its range goes too and the page name gives way entirely. A site with several scenes can then change scene only after widening.

Late fixes, same version: after a gesture is dropped by a selection or breakpoint change, the values it had shown are remembered per field only until its pointer and keys are released (one macrotask later) or the next `show()` or `commit()`, so a genuine choice of the same value on the newly selected element is never skipped. `parseRgb` refuses a space-separated `rgb()` with a fourth number and no slash, and a slash form with fewer than three channels, as CSS does (`args()` in `color-math.ts`).

Tests: `tests/unit/inspector/number.test.ts`, `color-math.test.ts`, `gradient-model.test.ts` and `grid-tracks.test.ts` go through the exported functions of the four value modules only, with expected values written by hand from CSS. `tests/window/test_design_controls.py` holds the rules every control must keep (typed values, one gesture one undo step, cut-off gestures, Delete and Backspace, a control opened while a value is in flight, first paint); `test_design_slider.py`, `test_design_color.py`, `test_design_gradient.py` and `test_design_grid.py` cover each control; `test_stage_path.py` covers the path above the canvas at five widths. They find controls by role and accessible name, so renaming "Choose {0}", "Type {0} by hand" or a slider's label fails them on purpose. A test that asserts a control is hidden first asserts it can be visible, because "hidden" is trivially true where the control does not exist. There is no test for the number patterns' speed: a timing test is not allowed by `docs/TESTING.md`.

## Import HTML files, the Page script, hover outlines and a faster desktop start · v0.32.0 (2026-11-26)

The first of five releases (0.32.0 to 0.36.0) that answer the follow-up on issue #36. `backend/HASHOUT_LOG.md`, same version, has the service side of the import (loose files, the new counting, the source-project refusal).

Import HTML files (`features/library/importer.ts`, `site-import.ts`, `index.ts`, `styles/library.css`): File > Import HTML files... (`file.import-html`, the id is unchanged, the label was Import HTML...) and File > Import site from zip... share one hidden `multiple` file input in `SiteImport`. `choose(kind)` only sets `accept` (`.html,.htm,text/html`, or `.zip,application/zip` plus those) and calls `click()`. The click must stay synchronous inside the command's run, because a browser opens a picker only inside the person's click or key press: never put an await or a timer before `choose()`. `importHtml()` shows the three-part import view (HTML files, A whole site in a .zip, Paste HTML code) behind the picker and moves to the review view through the `show` callback only once files are chosen, so cancelling leaves the person among the three ways in. `importSite()` still shows the review view behind its picker, because `test_convert.py` expects `.fw-lib-site-back` to be visible there. Loose files are read in the page and sent as `{"files": [{name, html}]}` to the same two routes as zip bytes (`request()` and `libraryMutate()` already send a plain object as JSON); a pick that mixes a zip with anything else, or holds another file type, is refused in the page with one sentence, and every refusal from the service is shown as sent. A picked file is read as bytes and decoded by `decode()` the way the service's `_text()` decodes a page inside a zip: strict UTF-8 (TextDecoder drops a byte order mark, as `utf-8-sig` does), else Windows-1252 with its five unassigned bytes (0x81, 0x8D, 0x8F, 0x90, 0x9D) as U+FFFD, as Python's `errors='replace'` makes them. It used `File.text()`, UTF-8 only, until review found that a Windows-1252 page lost every accented letter on this path and not in a zip; keep the two decoders in step, or the loose form and the zip stop giving the same page. When the review is refused, or finds no page, the files held in memory are dropped (`loose = null`), so neither As new pages nor As a section of this page can import a file the service has just refused; a new pick makes files importable again. A JSON body is somewhat larger than the files, so the service's 64 MiB cap can refuse what the page's own 64 MiB check let through. As a section of this page needs no review: it calls `importSection()` (exported from `importer.ts`) with the first file's text and its name without `.html`, and the paste part calls the same function, so the two cannot drift apart. The review scroller is a flex column; without `flex-shrink: 0` on its children the chosen-file list shrank by 5 px the moment the review made the scroller overflow, so keep that rule for any child added there. The chosen list keeps a 30 px minimum and the review comes after it, so nothing above it moves when the service answers.

Fixes after the feature run (same version). `choose()` calls `reset()` inside the same click, before the picker opens: files, review, result note and the Imported state are cleared and the view is scrolled to the top, so an earlier import's review is never left behind the picker and the next review appears once, in place (`read()` no longer clears a review on screen itself). `index.ts` `settle()` also resets a finished import when the person comes back to it (Back, the Import HTML tab clicked from another tab, the Library shown again). The result or refusal note lives at the end of the scroller, not in the footer, and work in progress ("Reading the archive...", "Importing...", "Converting...") is written on the import button (`progress()`), so the footer keeps one height and the note changes only once, to the result; after an import the note is scrolled into view with `block: 'nearest'`. The view mounts one Bring ... in as choice, between the review and the skipped list, whose heading `bringChoice().heading()` switches between Bring pages in as and Bring it in as; it serves pages and a section alike. Keep it single: `test_convert.py` reads `[data-bring="elements"]` inside `.fw-lib-site` with a strict locator, which fails when two match, and the skipped list sits in its own `.fw-lib-site-skipped` block so `.fw-lib-site-review` still matches one element. `importSection()` ends with "Undo once to get the section back as code, twice to remove it." and selects the converted root; the Library's `select` hook in `main.ts` now also calls `canvas.bring(id)`, so the new section is scrolled into view; the same hook serves every Library insert (`features/library/index.ts`), so a section inserted from the Library scrolls into view too. "The pages stayed code sections: {0}" still says pages on the section path, because `test_convert.py` checks that text on the paste path. The ignored count reads "{0} files in system and dependency folders are ignored."

Page script (`ui/canvas.ts`, `features/inspector/script-note.ts`, `styles/page-script.css`, `features/canvas-menu.ts`, the `script` icon in `ui/icons.ts`): a node is a Page script when its code section has JavaScript and no HTML, read from stored data by `codeKind(site, id)` (`'script'`, `'code'` or `''`), so sites imported before this version need no migration; the layer glyph, the canvas words, the inspector notes and the canvas menu's Design links all use that one helper. The text placeholder holds a title and a smaller line and carries `data-fw-poster="script|code|component"`; `syncPoster` writes the text nodes and the attribute only when they change. The script node is `display: contents` and has no box, so its placeholder is what you click and what shows the selection: only `[data-fw-poster=script]` takes pointer events (component and code placeholders stay `pointer-events: none`, so their clicks still fall through to the node with a box), `html[data-fw-dragging]` turns pointer events off on every placeholder so `elementFromPoint` cannot drop anything into the script, and `[data-fw-selected]>[data-fw-poster=script]` draws the selection outline. The dashed placeholder outline moved from the inline style into the canvas stylesheet, so neither needs `!important` and the reveal pulse still animates. `Canvas.find` falls back to the placeholder, so reveal and bring-into-view reach a node with no box. The Design and Motion panes are hidden by the class `fw-pane-script` plus one note per pane, not by setting `hidden` on each section, so inspector search and other code keep their own `hidden` state. The tab moves to Content only from Design or Motion, and goes back only if the person is still on Content when they select an ordinary element; the scroll position is restored in a microtask, before paint. The layer row icon holds all three glyphs (box, code, script) and switches with `data-glyph`; nothing is swapped through `innerHTML`. Not seen in a run: the text Component placeholder (every demo component has a poster picture), and the Menu key on a Page script (its anchor uses the node's box, and this node has none).

Hover outlines (`features/hover-outline.ts`, `styles/hover-outline.css`, `view.hover-outline`): the marker is the attribute `data-fw-hover` on at most one canvas node and one layer row, never a class, so exact-class assertions and `keyed()` updates keep working, and `ui/canvas.ts` never removes it because the canvas removes only attributes it set itself. The iframe rule lives in a `<style>` the module adds after `canvas.loaded()`, as `canvas-drag` and `canvas-menu` do. It leaves out selected, marked, lifted and page-root nodes, and every node while `html` has `data-fw-dragging` or `data-fw-holding`, so the selection outline always wins, even before the next pointer move clears the hover. The colour is the canvas's literal orange at 70 % alpha (`#f97316b3`, 1 px, offset 1 px), because the editor tokens do not exist inside the iframe; Chrome rounds outline widths to device pixels, so 1.5 px drew as 1 px anyway. A Page script is outlined through its `[data-fw-poster=script]` child. `blocked()` runs once per animation frame: the switch off, Preview or Preview as published, the dialog scrim, the Quick insert flyout, any `.fw-menu` that is not `hidden` (menubar popups stay in the DOM, hidden), a `contenteditable` node, and the body drag classes of both drag modules. Page changes are caught by a MutationObserver on the Layers list's children, comparing the page root with the one the hover was drawn for, because `openPage` has no hook and the old page's nodes stay in the iframe, hidden; site changes clear through `subscribe()`. Only `pointerType === 'mouse'` draws, so touch and pen never leave a stale hover. The switch is kept in `localStorage` `formweb.canvas.hover-outline` and is on unless the stored value is `'0'`. The Layers list element is passed in directly: `#fw-layers-list` is set by `features/panel/fold.ts`, not by `main.ts`.

Desktop start-up (`src-tauri/src/reveal.rs`, `start_times.rs`, `main.rs`): `SPLASH_MIN` is 500 ms, was 2600 ms; `BAR_FINISH` (220 ms) and `FALLBACK` (20 s) are unchanged. In practice the window shows when the page calls `app_ready` (from `revealApp`, three frames after first paint) plus `BAR_FINISH`, so "window shown" is normally "editor ready" plus about 220 ms; `BAR_FINISH` is the next knob if that gap should go, at the cost of a splash that closes on a partly filled bar. `public/splash.html` was left alone on purpose, because it matches formvis line for line for the family hand-off: its bar still runs to 92 % over 2.6 s, so a fast start sweeps it from about 30 % to full during `BAR_FINISH` (measured in Chromium at 4x CPU: it rises steadily and never jumps back), and a slow start holds it at 92 % until ready. `start_times.rs` keeps four `OnceLock<Duration>` moments, all measured from `reveal::mark_launch()`, the first line of `main()`, and recorded once: service started (right after the sidecar or source service spawns), service ready (just before the editor window is opened, after the health and window-key waits, or at once when a running service of this version is adopted), editor ready (in `app_ready`) and window shown (in `show_main`, after `main.show()`). `show_main` prints the line once, after the splash closes, with `writeln!` to stderr whose error is ignored, so a missing or closed stderr can never panic: `[formweb] start-up: service started 120 ms, service ready 2140 ms, editor ready 2630 ms, window shown 2850 ms`. A moment that never happened reads `<name> not reached` (the 20 s fallback gives `editor ready not reached`), and an adopted service reads `service already running`, inferred from ready being set while started is not. When "service ready" is close to "editor ready", the remaining cost is the service; on Windows that is expected to be the one-file sidecar unpacking itself, between "service started" and "service ready", which this wave did not change. `line()` is a pure function, ready for a test. Checked: `cargo check` and `cargo check --tests` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, and `start_times.rs` in a scratch crate for all four forms of the line. Its six unit tests ran only in such a scratch crate that includes the real file: CI runs `cargo test` only in `native/`, and `./formweb test` does not build `src-tauri`, so they guard nothing until a runner with the GTK and WebKit development packages runs `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml`. Their expected strings come from the line as first written, not from a document. Not run: the shell on Linux (its WebKitGTK and GTK system libraries are missing here) and the desktop app on any system, so the real timing and the line inside a real window are unverified. `docs/DESKTOP_TESTING.md` has the owner's steps.

## The window keeps the newest state when its own answer arrives late · v0.31.2 (2026-11-25)

`api.ts` has two ways for site state to reach the window: the answer to a request the window made, and the `state` event on `/api/events`. The event path has always refused anything that is not newer (`incoming.revision > current.revision`, or another site). The answer path called `accept()` with no check. The service sends the event before it returns the answer, so the usual order is event first, answer second, both at the same revision, which is harmless. The harmful order is: the event for the window's own change, then the event for a change from outside the window, then the answer to the window's own change. The answer is one revision behind, `accept()` took it, and nothing came afterwards to correct it, because the event that would have was already consumed. The window then showed the older site (status bar one revision behind, canvas and panels without the outside change) until the next change of any kind.

`settle(next)` is the answer path's guard: it returns the state the window already has when `next` is the same site (`site.id`) at a lower revision, and otherwise calls `accept(next)`. `command()`, `commands()`, `mutateVia()` and `libraryMutate()` use it. Three callers keep plain `accept()` on purpose: `replace()` (open, new, restore and branch switches are allowed to lower the revision, since the engine restores the stored revision on load), the refetch of `/api/state` after a 409, and `connect()`. A caller of `command()` that reads the returned state gets the newer state in the late case, which is what it should act on.

The engine raises the revision by one for every command, undo and redo, and lowers it only on load and restore, which is why a lower revision of the same site from a command answer can only be a late answer.

Tests: `tests/unit/api/newest-state.test.ts` drives `api.ts` with a stand-in `fetch` whose answers are released by the test and a stand-in `EventSource`; two of its six cases fail without `settle()`. `tests/window/test_newest_state.py` does the same in the real window by holding the answer to the window's own insert with a Playwright route until an outside command has reached the status bar; it failed three runs of three before the fix. `test_unstyled_text_has_the_same_font_on_the_canvas_and_in_the_exported_page` in `test_second_round.py`, which failed once in CI at 0.31.1 with `rev 3` shown where `rev 4` was expected, hit this order by chance: it inserts through the window and sends an outside `node.update` straight after.

## Issue #36 closed ask by ask: site background, layout grid, editable imports, crumbs and the last fixes · v0.31.1 (2026-11-25)

The final pull request for issue #36: the report was split into 49 asks and each was checked in the app; this release closes the last ones. It is `main` at 0.31.0 plus small fixes and the isolation of local publications (`backend/HASHOUT_LOG.md`, same version), then three stacked branches (site background and page menu; layout grid; editable imports), then three review fixes and the release. `native/HASHOUT_LOG.md` has the engine side.

Site background (`features/inspector/page-background.ts`): the shared background is the design token `--page-background` and a page is on it when its root's `background` is exactly `var(--page-background)`. It is a token and not a model field because a sister app opens `.fweb` files with an older engine that refuses unknown fields; the exporter already writes tokens into `:root` and the canvas already sets them, so no Rust changed. The group derives its state from the site (Site background, Same as another page, This page only); Use the site background on all pages is one batch (`token.set` once, then every root's `node.update`), Add page's Background choice comes from `startChoices` and `startCommands` (a new page and its look in one batch), and `readableTextPatch` (`features/contrast.ts`) adds or takes back `color: #ffffff` in the same `node.update`, offered in a notice with Undo, and leaves sites with themes alone. A token removed from the site is now removed from the canvas document too, so a page on a deleted site background shows white as the export does.

Starter styles (`features/starter-elements.ts`): `starterStyles(site)` gives Insert and Quick insert elements that take their colors from the page (no section background, translucent lines, `color: inherit` on links and fields, a muted Paragraph); a Button is filled with `--accent` and its text is black or white from the shared contrast helper. On themed sites that text color is judged against the default theme only (known gap).

Layout grid (`features/layout-grid.ts` geometry, `features/layout-layer.ts` drawing, wired in `canvas-drag.ts`): slots, lanes, guides and gap measures are read from the page's real flow, measured once per container per drag, drawn from pooled fixed elements in `#overlay-root` with `pointer-events: none`, and cleared on drop, Escape, any key, a page change and leaving design. Nothing in it relates to breakpoints or the canvas width: CLAUDE.md's canvas law forbids deriving a breakpoint from the canvas width and drawing breakpoint markers, and the grid is the flow of the breakpoint the person chose, nothing more. `view.layout-outlines` draws the same layer without a drag and is kept in `localStorage`, never in the site. Review fix: over a container that is a sibling of the dragged element, `findDrop` now uses a quarter of its size along the flow as the before and after band (12 px for any other container). A reviewer dragged the third card of a converted flex row onto the first and the drop went inside the first card ("Element moved", row order unchanged); reproduced in Chromium with trusted CDP input and covered by `test_convert.py`.

Converter (`features/convert/`): the section is measured in two hidden frames, the original and a bare twin, each `sandbox="allow-same-origin"` without `allow-scripts`, with the policy set both by a first `<meta http-equiv>` and by the frame's `csp` attribute (no network except the site's own `/api/assets/` pictures and `data:`). Computed styles are reduced against the bare twin's defaults so only what differs is written, base styles only, never a breakpoint. One conversion is at most 480 elements and goes to the engine as one batch of at most 4,000 commands, so the engine's own checks decide; a site import converts its pages together up to that batch and a page too large alone stays code. An import as elements is two undo steps: the import, then the conversion. The result sentence now adds "Check it at phone width in Preview, then adjust under each breakpoint." whenever responsive rules were left out.

Smaller: `features/crumbs.ts` folds the middle of a long selection path into one "..." crumb whose menu uses `ui/context-menu.ts` raw (untranslated) entries; the page menu adds Preview, Add page and Page background on the same `showContextMenu`; Audit Select puts its sentence in the shared notice with Show again; the dead `preview` parameter of `canvas.render` is removed; Show in folder's stable shell codes (`download.none`, `download.moved` and `download.reveal-failed` from `src-tauri/src/downloads.rs`, with the file, folder and system reason) become sentences in every language; the editor treats addresses in other capitals as taken, as the engine does.

Lesson: `git stash` is shared by every worktree of a repository, because stashes live in the common `.git`; one agent on this release lost work to that. Never stash in a shared repository; commit to the branch or copy the files out.

## Issue #36: what a new user asked for, and the editor work that answers it · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

The first outside contributor report (issue #36) listed what a person coming from a desktop builder missed: menus on pages and layers, renaming in place, dragging in Layers and on the canvas, several things selected at once, finding a property, a preview of the finished site, a page that looked black but published white, an Export that seemed to do nothing, an Audit Select that went nowhere visible, importing a site, and a guide. The work was done in slices on stacked branches and shipped as four releases, one per pull request: 0.28.1 (export notice, audit Select, File > Import HTML), 0.29.0 (the Pages and layers panel, Quick insert, page background, `page.move`), 0.30.0 (multi-select, the canvas menu and drag, property search, Preview as published, `/api/paste` fragments) and 0.31.0 (everything else: site import, the guide, touch and pen, Select several, the desktop run and its fixes, the canvas script rules, the second round of fixes, the translations and the tests). This entry and the ones below record the whole of that work; the 0.28.1 to 0.30.0 changelog sections say what each of those releases carried. Command keys added: `Mod+Shift+L` (`insert.quick`), `Mod+Alt+F` (`inspector.search`), `Mod+Shift+P` (`view.preview-full`), and `Backspace` beside `Delete` on `edit.delete`; `edit.select-mode` has no key. Not run: anything on Windows or macOS (their shell code was compiled for them, not run), touch on a real device (only a browser's touch emulation), and the translations were not read by native speakers.

## Export says what it saved, and the canvas brings a node into view · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`ui/notice.ts`: one notice at the bottom right in the overlay root, long enough to read, with an optional action. `ui/downloads.ts` keeps a button busy while the file is made (the busy state keeps the button's size and pulses its icon, still under reduced motion), then names the file and where it went; a refusal of the service is said in words. `api.ts` `download()` now attaches its link to the document and keeps the blob URL for 60 s: both are defensive fixes for suspected causes of the silent failure, not proven ones. Export and Download .fweb project use the same path.

`ui/canvas.ts`: `reveal(id)` centres a node and pulses it for two seconds, `bring(id)` scrolls only when it is out of view. The pulse animates `outline-offset`, because the selection outline is `!important`, and uses the canvas document's existing hex colour, because tokens are not available inside the iframe. Audit Select calls `reveal`, scrolls the layer row into view only when it is hidden and keeps the issue in the status line; a page issue opens Page settings and flashes its field. Picking a layer or a breadcrumb calls `bring`. Starting a text edit with a double click never scrolls the canvas.

The canvas default colours: the canvas iframe body had `color:#edf0f5;background:#15171b`, so every page without a background of its own looked dark while the export has only `body{margin:0}` and shows white. It now has `color-scheme:light` with `CanvasText` and `Canvas`. All eight demos set their own root background, so none changed. The canvas no longer sets Inter either (second round, below), so unstyled text has the browser's default font in both.

## Pages and layers: menus, rename, fold, drag, Quick insert and the home page · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`ui/context-menu.ts` (`showContextMenu`, `menuKeyPressed`, `anchorOf`) builds the page menu, the layer menu (refactored onto it with the same items and classes) and the canvas menu, with separators, key hints, arrow-key navigation, and the Menu key or Shift+F10 at the focused row. The panel modules are `features/panel/{index,pages,rename,fold,drop,drag,insert-flyout}.ts`. Rows stay `<button>`s inside `keyed()` lists, so the rename field and the drop line are fixed elements in the overlay root; the rename field follows its row when the list changes under it and closes when the row goes away. The twisty is placed with `grid-column: 1`, so the row child order main.ts depends on is unchanged. Folding is stored only in localStorage, never in the site, because FormPublisher opens .fweb files with an older vendored engine. Dragging uses pointer events, not HTML5 drag, so Escape, auto-scroll near the edges and the threshold are ours; a layer dropped into a folded container unfolds it. Drop rules (`drop.ts` `dropTarget`, `moveCommands`) refuse page roots, a node into its own descendants, non-containers and the structure of a linked instance; a drop is one batch, one undo step.

Page commands (`page.open`, `page.rename`, `page.duplicate`, `page.set-home`, `page.move-up`, `page.move-down`, `layer.rename`) take an optional `id`; `invoke()` runs them for a page other than the open one, because a command's `enabled` cannot see params. Page order goes through the new engine command `page.move` (native log). Set as home page rewrites slugs (the page gets `/`, the old home gets an address from its name) and links, in one batch, and refuses for a CMS template page, a translated address, or a page a password gate protects through a route other than `/`: gates are keyed by route in the service database, so moving a gated page to `/` would have made it public.

Quick insert (`insert-flyout.ts`) knows no site data: main.ts passes the groups (elements, sections, components) and their run functions, so it cannot drift from the Insert panel; its last row opens the Insert panel. Combobox and listbox pattern with `aria-activedescendant`; Tab closes it back to the plus button. Starter pages are left out on purpose.

## Several selected at once, the canvas menu and dragging on the canvas · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`features/selection.ts` and `selection-actions.ts`: `selectedId` in main.ts stays the primary, and the module falls back to that one element whenever other code changes `selectedId`, so no single-selection code changed. Existing command ids branch when more than one item is selected; there are no parallel commands. Ctrl/Cmd+click, Shift+click, Shift+arrows and Ctrl/Cmd+A work in the Pages and Layers lists; on the canvas Ctrl, Cmd or Shift click adds or removes an element (Shift+click no longer paints a text selection). Non-primary canvas outlines use `data-fw-marked` (dashed). `topMost` leaves page roots out. Copy and Cut refuse more than 64 elements, the most one paste takes. The selection reads as empty until the engine is connected, so menus built at start do not throw. The inspector identity reads how many are selected and names the one the controls edit, on one line.

`features/canvas-menu.ts`: listeners are attached from the parent to the frame's document; Edit text sends a double click so the canvas's own text editing runs unchanged; positions are converted from the frame rectangle and scale; copied styles are kept in this window only, and Paste styles skips the brand restriction because the values come from elements already in the site. Right-clicking a selected element keeps the selection and the labels count what each action changes. The field jumps (`inspector.text-style`, `background`, `spacing`, `size`, `link`, `image`) are registered commands.

`features/canvas-drag.ts`: candidates come from DOM rectangles and the parent's computed flex or grid flow, each validated with the same `dropTarget` and `moveCommands` as Layers, so canvas and Layers share one rule set. No x/y offsets are written and nothing is drawn at breakpoint positions. One style element in the iframe head sets cursor, user-select and dimming only. A 6 px threshold on a selected element, an opaque accent proxy, auto-scroll near the top and bottom, Escape to cancel; the release after a cancelled or refused drag selects nothing, and the menu does not open during a drag. After a drag the selection WebKit left from the press is cleared, so no text stays highlighted.

## Property search, Preview as published, page background, site import, the guide · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`features/inspector/search.ts` (with `search-terms.ts` for synonym groups), as it is since 0.35.0: the index is built from the mounted DOM (`data-section`, `data-attr`, the existing `data-style` and `data-field`) of the Design, Motion, Content and Page panes, not from a field list. Hidden controls are left out; disabled ones are listed as Unavailable. A MutationObserver only marks the index stale. Results are a sibling of `.inspector-scroll`, swapped with `hidden`, and the scroll position comes back on clear. A field is named by its label text only, never the text of a button inside the label (the Page background label held one). The search is a list only: rows are `role="listitem"` text in a `role="list"` ("{name} · {tab} > {section}", a choice adds " > {field}"; the separators and names are set as text with `data-no-i18n`), a note above the list says "Each result shows where to find it. Open that tab and section to use it.", and nothing in the module switches a tab, unfolds a section, scrolls the inspector, focuses a control or sets a class or attribute outside its own bar and results; the combobox roles, the active row, Up, Down and Enter, the row click handlers, the group headings and the `fw-search-pulse` outline of earlier versions are gone. Choices are read from the mounted panes like fields: `.fw-seg-btn` (`data-value` is a search word), the options of `select[data-style]` (the empty Inherit option is skipped) and segment groups outside any field (Motion presets; a `.field-label` just before such a group names its field level). They match only by their own name or value, or by their place when another word of the query matched the name, so "weight 800" finds 800 but "weight" alone does not list every weight. A choice never sorts above its own field: its score is raised to the field's when the field is listed, and index order breaks the tie; results keep each section's rows together, sorted by the section's best score. A choice carries its field's off state (or its own `disabled`), so it is Unavailable where its field is. A mousedown inside the results (not on the scrollbar) is prevented, so a click on text keeps focus in the box and never drops it on the body; Escape with text clears, and Escape on an empty box moves focus to the active tab button (it has `data-own-keys`) instead of `blur()`, which dropped focus on the body. Bounds: 4,000 entries, 64 choices per group or select, names cut at 120 characters, the query at most 120 characters (`maxLength`), 120 rows shown. Ranking uses `includes` and `startsWith` on normalized words; no regular expression is built from the query.

`features/full-preview.ts` with the shared double-buffered `features/compiled-frames.ts`; `RuntimePreview` is now a thin wrapper over the same pair. The eye button's `data-cmd` is `view.preview-full`; `view.preview` keeps its id and menu item and has a Back to design button in its frame label. While open, keys are held by a window capture listener, the command gate allows only `view.preview-full`, and siblings are `inert`. Escape typed inside the previewed page goes to that page (opaque sandbox). It follows each new revision, swapping only when the new build has loaded. Breakpoint names on the width buttons are the site's own words, never translated.

`features/inspector/page-background.ts`: the site model has no site background, so "site background" means the browser default (white). The group always writes base (desktop) styles of the page root. A value the browser cannot read as a background (`CSS.supports('background', ...)` after tokens are resolved) is refused, there and before Apply to all pages. Page background... opens its section if folded and scrolls the whole group into view so the heading stays visible.

`features/library/site-import.ts`: the review call is on the `api.ts` wait list next to `/api/audit`, and the import goes through `libraryMutate`. File > Import HTML... (`file.import-html`) opens the Library on its Import HTML tab; File > Import site from zip... (`file.import-site`) leaves the Home screen and opens the review. Help > Using FormWeb starts with Open the full guide, a link to `docs/guide` on GitHub.

Fixes made after the user guide was written, found while taking its pictures: New site from Blank, from the dialog or Home, sends `blank: true` to `/api/new` (before, only a starter did, so Blank opened the sample home page). The canvas document has `body>[hidden]{display:none!important}`, so a page that is not open stays hidden even when its root sets `display` (the five starters drew an earlier page over the open one). The property search label reading above. The Page background scroll and validation above. Plurals: the audit summary says "1 issue found" and the site import review "1 stylesheet or script". `Backspace` also deletes the selected element (Mac laptops have no Delete key); `commands/keymap.ts` does not run it while typing in a field or editing text.

## Touch and pen, and Select several · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`features/hold.ts` is the one long-press helper the row drags (`features/panel/drag.ts`) and the canvas drag share: `HOLD_MS` 350 ms of holding still within `SLOP` 8 px lifts a row or element for a touch drag, so an ordinary swipe still scrolls and a tap still selects. A pen drags like the mouse after the usual threshold, and `startsDrag` refuses a pen press with the barrel button held (`buttons !== 1`), the barrel rule. `blockScroll` puts one permanent `touchmove` listener (`passive: false`) on the list and on the canvas document and calls `preventDefault` only while a drag is armed: `touch-action` set after the finger is down does not affect that gesture, so the listener has to exist before it starts. `hushMenus` holds back the system's own context menu during a hold and a drag; a hold released without moving calls `openMenu`, which sends a `contextmenu` event at the point, so the existing page, layer and canvas menus open by touch with no new menu code. A second finger, or `pointercancel` when the system takes the touch over, cancels the drag with nothing moved.

`features/select-mode.ts` with the `edit.select-mode` command (Edit > Select several and a toggle in the Pages and Layers headings, in space the headings reserve, `styles/select-mode.css`): while it is on, a tap or click adds an item to the selection or takes it out as Ctrl+click does, the headings show "3 selected" and Done in place of their titles, and a double tap does not rename or edit text. Done, Escape or the toggle ends it and keeps the selection. It lives in `selection.ts` as the `picking` state, so no single-selection code changed. Off a Mac, Ctrl+click on the canvas, a layer or a page never opens a menu, so it only adds to the selection.

## The canvas may run the editor's listeners, and nothing from the site · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

Found on the first desktop run: WebKitGTK runs no event listener in a frame sandboxed without `allow-scripts`, not even the parent's, so the canvas took no clicks, double-clicks, right-clicks or drags in the desktop app. The canvas frame is now sandboxed `allow-same-origin allow-scripts`, and three rules keep site content from running there. The canvas document's Content-Security-Policy is `default-src 'none'` with images, media, inline styles and fonts only and `form-action 'none'`, so no script runs and no form is sent. No element on the canvas is a hyperlink: an `a` keeps its address in `data-fw-href`, and only the in-canvas live preview follows it, as a scroll to an anchor or an editor page switch, so no link opens a page inside the frame. The frame's Navigation API `navigate` event is cancelled for every navigation, so nothing can replace the document that carries the policy. The renderer itself builds only allowlisted tags and attributes with `createElement`, `createElementNS` and `textContent` (an unknown tag becomes a `div`, `href`, `src` and `poster` must be safe addresses, and no `data-fw-*` attribute but `data-fw-theme-only` is taken from the site), so the policy is a second wall, not the only one. `docs/DESKTOP_TESTING.md` records the WebKitGTK run before and after the fix; the wiki says why the frame may run scripts.

## The second round: what the guide and the tests turned up · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

The width note above the canvas has a two-line place of its own in `styles/stage-head.css`, so a long selection path shortens instead of hiding it. A site with no themes says so: Default theme reads None and the canvas theme buttons are off and unpressed (`features/inspector/theme.ts`), as in the export. `canon('+')` in `commands/registry.ts` keeps `+` as its own key instead of reading it as the separator, so Add Anchor Point lists `+` and its label reads `+`. A new image has no `alt` until it is described, so the audit asks for one; `features/decorative-image.ts` adds Decorative image (no description), which writes `alt=""` on purpose and turns the text field off, and clearing the text removes the attribute. Undo, Redo, a rename and a token change say what they did on the status line, which keeps its full text in its tooltip; Send to FormPublisher shortens the inbox path in the middle. Import HTML explains itself in plain words, with the technical note about code sections and kit helpers as its tooltip. The scene scrubber's 0, 50, 100 and Off stay inside the bar. The Help menu item reads Using FormWeb, the dialog's own title. Add page (`features/page-slug.ts`) fills the slug from the name until it is edited, shows the address it will use (the path alone while the base URL is the example.com placeholder) and refuses a taken or unusable address before closing. The Library tabs wrap a long label instead of clipping it, and the open tab no longer turns bold and wider. The canvas no longer sets Inter, so text with no font of its own has the browser's default font, as the export does. Property search lists at most 120 results and then says so in a last row that is not selectable. Copying several elements that could never be pasted together (over the service's 8,000 node total, backend log) is refused with the same sentence and the clipboard kept.

## Translations and the tests · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

i18n: the 223 interface strings added from 0.28.1 to 0.31.0 are appended to `i18n/keys.ts` and to all 23 full tables, machine translated and not read by native speakers; `es-MX` overrides nine of them with its own wording and `fr-CA` none. Template literals that built a sentence at run time are routed through `t(...)` with `{0}` placeholders at the call sites this work touched (`features/panel/index.ts`, `features/panel/pages.ts`, `features/canvas-drag.ts`, `features/inspector/page-background.ts`, `features/runtime-preview.ts`, and in main.ts the token, delete page and audit summary sentences). Proper names (the Home badge, breakpoint names) stay untranslated. `i18n/family/` is untouched, because it is a shared copy of formvis's tables. `./formweb i18n check` passes.

Tests: vitest 5.0.5 and happy-dom 21.0.4 are development packages only (none of them is in the built editor; `LICENSES.md` lists them), running `frontend/tests/unit` through `npm test`, `./formweb test unit` and CI. The real window is driven by Playwright in `frontend/tests/window`, with fixtures that start a fresh engine per test, open an editor page and send trusted pointer input to the canvas, including touch through Chromium's touch emulation. `docs/TESTING.md` says how to run each layer.

## The desktop shell saves downloads itself, opens links in the system browser, and was run on Linux · v0.31.0 (2026-11-25)

`src-tauri/src/downloads.rs`: the download handler now runs on Windows too, so WebView2's own download flyout no longer appears and the editor's notice replaces it. Every download goes to Downloads without a prompt, under the name the shell really wrote. macOS reports no path at Finished, so the shell keeps the requested destination per URL. The saved path is told only to the editor page at `http://127.0.0.1:<ENGINE_PORT>`. `reveal_download` takes no arguments and opens only the last path the shell recorded; the capability change adds only `allow-reveal-download`. Linux opens the folder with xdg-open and does not select the file.

Found and fixed on the first run (Linux, WebKitGTK, under a virtual display): the shell plugin was removed (`Cargo.toml`, `main.rs`), because its page script swallowed every `target=_blank` click, so Open in browser and the guide link did nothing; new-window links now reach the system browser through the shell's own handler. On Linux without a Downloads entry in `~/.config/user-dirs.dirs`, WebKitGTK saved into the folder the app was started from; such a file is now moved to `~/Downloads`, made if missing. Show in folder for a file that was moved or deleted turns the notice red and names the file and its folder. On Windows `explorer /select,` gets the quoted path through `raw_arg`, so a folder with a comma or no space still selects the file. The service revalidates the editor page on every load (backend log), so after an update the new editor shows at once. `docs/DESKTOP_TESTING.md` holds the hand checks for each system and the table of what was run: on Linux the debug shell and the `.deb` from `./build_app`, both passed; the Windows and macOS code was compiled with `cargo check` for their targets and not run. Known: after one failed download, WebKitGTK reports every later download as failed too, a fault in the web view library.

## Five whole-site starters, a site type per demo, and the preview packed with eight sites · v0.28.0 (2026-11-22)

Owner's direction: FormPublisher starts a person's site from FormWeb's whole-site demos, and three were too few. Five starters are added, one per common kind of site, each in `public/demos/<id>/` with `site.fweb`, `cover.webp` and `demo.json`: `coldmere-plumbing` (business: home, services, about, contact, 404), `northfold-studio` (portfolio: home, work, four project pages from the `projects` collection through a template page, about, contact, 404), `slow-allotment` (blog: home with the three newest posts, posts, three posts from the `posts` collection, about, 404), `wenlow-type-day` (event: home, programme, speakers from the `speakers` collection, venue and travel, tickets with no checkout, 404) and `fernhollow-pottery` (shop window: home, shop, six product pages from the `products` collection, about, how to order by email, 404).

How they were made: each through the running engine's HTTP API, not by writing project files. `POST /api/new {name, blank: true}`, then `POST /api/assets` for the fonts (the same woff2 files the three demos store, byte for byte) and the pictures, then `/api/batch` batches: `site.update` (name, `baseUrl` on a `.example` address, `favicon`), `token.set`, `design.set fonts`, `page.add` and `page.update` (title under 60 characters, description under 160, the 404 page `noindex`), `collection.create` and `cms.upsert`, `node.add` for every element, `component.create` of the header and footer on Home and `component.insert` on every other page, `cms.query` and `cms.binding`, `extension.set cms` for the templates, `extension.set behaviors` for hover looks (copied to each linked instance node) and `extension.set code` for the head. The project was then saved with `GET /api/project/download`, loaded back without its history (`POST /api/load` of the envelope less `checkpoint`, which clears undo as the three demos have it) and downloaded again: that second file is `site.fweb`. Every page root is a `div` holding `header`, `main` and `footer`, so the landmarks are siblings. The builder scripts are not part of this repository.

Things the engine does not offer and how the starters handle them: there is no Open Graph image setting, so the site head code carries `og:image` as an absolute address on the base URL (`<base>/assets/<sha>.png`, where the plain export writes that asset), its size and alt text, `og:site_name`, `twitter:card` and `theme-color`, plus `<link rel="image_src" href="/api/assets/<sha>.png">`, because the export bundles an asset only when the document names its `/api/assets/` address. A person who changes the base URL must change that address too; MANUAL says so. A CMS binding sets text, `src` or `href` only (`formweb-model` `advanced.rs`), never `alt`, so a picture bound to a collection field has `alt=""`: on cards the linked title next to it names the item, and on project and product pages a `figcaption` bound to a caption field describes the picture. One node takes one binding, so a card link bound to `href` carries its bound title in a child `span`. No starter has a `form`; contact is a `mailto:` link, and the shop's order button is a per-product `mailto:` with the product in the subject, kept in a `url` field.

`ui/demos.ts`: `DemoInfo` gains `type: DemoType` (`business`, `portfolio`, `blog`, `event`, `shop`), `demo()` takes it as its fourth argument, and `DEMOS` lists eight. Lowlight and Harbour Ferries are `business`, Looking Back `portfolio`. The same three fields are in each folder's `demo.json` (`{"name", "description", "type"}`, the description being the card's line), for tools that read the folder: formai-templates' collector reads a demo's metadata from such a file. The five lines are new keys appended to `i18n/keys.ts` and to the 23 full tables by hand; the titles are proper names and stay untranslated.

`public/demos/preview.html`: packed again with the eight sites. `fwpack.py` is not in this repository; the new file was made by a script that writes the fwpack 1.0 format (asset paths in pages and style sheets become `fwpack-asset:` tokens, unreferenced style sheets are dropped, binary files are base64 with their type). Run on fresh exports of the three older demos it gives their three blocks byte for byte, and those blocks are left as they were. The five new blocks follow them, with a tab each in the bar. Changed in the shell: the bar scrolls sideways without a visible scrollbar (`.fwp-bar` gets `overflow-x:auto;scrollbar-width:none`), since eight tabs pass the width of a 1024 px window. For a site without themes the block's `themeKey` is null, `theme` light and `bg` the paper colour in both modes.

Checked: each starter exported (`GET /api/export`, the plain export Send to FormPublisher uses) and imported into a scratch copy of FormPublisher 0.9.0 with a fresh `FORMPUBLISHER_HOME` (`run import <zip> --json --in-process`, then `run check <id> --json --in-process --deep`, static and browser tiers at 1440 and 390): 0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos for all five. Earlier rounds found and fixed: a home title of 71 characters (`meta.title`), cards on the work page jumping from h1 to h3 (`heading.order`), and card titles lost because a second binding on the same link replaced the first. FormWeb's audit: no issue on any of the five. Each `site.fweb` loaded back into the engine gives the same page, node, component and collection counts and an export identical file for file; every `/api/assets/` address in each document has its asset in the envelope. Home pages rendered at 1280 and 390 px with no horizontal overflow and no console error. WCAG contrast of every text and background token pair 5.1:1 or more. The built editor at 1440 x 900: Home shows eight cards, Preview shows a starter with its tab, and a starter card opens the site (Fernhollow Pottery, six pages). `./formweb i18n check` passes; `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0.

Known: the share picture's address must follow a changed base URL by hand. The preview of the three older demos was not packed again. Not run: Windows, macOS, the desktop shell.

## The token file carries the family's names · after v0.26.0 (2026-11-20)

`src/styles/tokens.css` · the family's design alignment (decisions 1 to 5 of the design pass): FormVis's token names are the family's names. Measured against FormVis `frontend/src/styles/tokens.css` before this change: 85 of FormVis's 154 names were here, 69 were missing, and of the 85 only six had another value: `--font-ui` and five z-index values.

- Added, with FormVis Studio Dark's values: the 50 names FormWeb had no token for (node and canvas, the `--handle-*` colours, `--transition-normal`, `--text-on-cta`, `--accent-glow`, `--ok-soft`, `--danger-text`, the success and danger fills, `--brand-orange`, the `--cv-*` layout and canvas names, the `--glass-*` names). None of them is read by any FormWeb stylesheet, script or the shared panel today, so nothing on screen changes.
- `--titlebar-h` is now an alias of `--cv-titlebar-h` (both 40px, FormWeb's value and FormVis's are the same). The other own names that have a FormVis counterpart already were aliases (`--accent-text`, `--danger-color`, `--success-color`, `--space`, `--radius-small`, `--radius-large`, `--control-height`); the rest have none and stay as they are.
- Kept: the accent; the base font size; `--font-ui`, whose only difference is `var(--font-script)`, the bundled Han, Hangul and Thai fallbacks that the language tables need (a font stack is not among the surface, text, border, radius and shadow values that take FormVis's); the z-index scale, with its five shared names at FormWeb's values (`--z-raised` 100, `--z-chrome` 300, `--z-overlay` 600, `--z-toast` 800, `--z-debug` 1000) and its own names (`--z-sticky`, `--z-dropdown`, `--z-scrim`, `--z-popover`, `--z-tooltip`); `--z-base` is 0 in both. The 19 z names FormVis has and FormWeb lacks are not added: their values belong to FormVis's scale (`--z-modal` 1000, `--z-overlay-modal` 60000) and would stack wrongly against FormWeb's (above its tooltip at 900), and nothing here reads them.
- Light and AMOLED: FormWeb's theme table equals FormVis's in every value FormWeb sets (FormVis's adds `--node-shadow` per profile, which FormWeb does not read). FormVis's light layer sets 12 names, all here with the same light values. FormWeb's light layer also sets `--danger-soft` and `--ok` darker for contrast on light surfaces; they are status colours, not among the names that take FormVis's, and stay.

Checked, before and after, with a built window served by the engine at 1440x900: the editor and the splash page in dark and in light, 0 pixels differ in all four pairs; the resolved value of every custom property on the root (172 names before, 222 after): no value changed, 50 names added, none gone (the splash page reads no tokens); a cold load of the editor under 4x CPU throttle, dark and light: 6 frames each, the only white one is the first, the blank tab before the request, the same as before the change; no console error in dark, the same 4 in light before and after. The pre-paint theme script in `index.html` is untouched. Raw `z-index` numbers and colour literals outside the token file: unchanged (only this file changed). `npx vite build`: exit 0.

## The two Agent Connect sentences name all four programs and say what each reads · after v0.26.0 (2026-11-20)

`src/features/agent-connect.ts` :: AgentConnect (the Clients note), AgentConnect.setAgents (the Agents off note), `src/i18n/keys.ts` (the two keys, in place, so every table keeps its order) and the 23 full tables in `src/i18n/locales/` · the old sentences named Codex and Cursor only, and the Clients note said that they "read the project file, which starts ./formweb mcp and needs Agents on". That was no longer what Open does: each of the four Open buttons starts the program in a dock tab with an in-app token of its own (`backend/app/agent_programs.py` `launch`), and `/mcp` lets that token through with Agents off (`backend/app/main.py`). What each reads differs: Claude Code and Codex are given the server when they start, Cursor reads `.cursor/mcp.json` in FormWeb's agent folder (`cursor_config`), and Antigravity reads `.agents/mcp_config.json` in a folder of the tab's own (`tab_folder`, `write_tab_entry`). A program started outside FormWeb reads its project file (`.mcp.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`), or for Antigravity the account file `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` (`backend/app/clients.py`), whose entry starts `formweb mcp` and is refused while Agents are off. The new sentences say exactly that, with the four in the family's order. `es-MX` and `fr-CA` override neither key. Proved by the engine test of `backend/HASHOUT_LOG.md` (same date). Translated here; no native speaker has read them. Checked: `./formweb i18n check`, exit 0, 2566 keys and 2566 entries in each of the 23 tables.

## The panel module 2.2.1: its guide names Codex, Cursor and Antigravity · after v0.25.1 (2026-11-20)

`src/dock/` · every file copied unchanged from FormVis commit 23b018892caa30eae5654a2f98611bd81f0f77ad (`MANIFEST.json` included); three files differ from 2.2.0 (`CONTRACT.md`, `MANIFEST.json`, `strings.ts`). `setup/agents.py` `DOCK_VERSION` is 2.2.1. `src/dock-adapter.ts` · FormWeb's own `tourReadyBody` gets the module's new sentence: "Codex, Cursor and Antigravity each get a button of their own here when they are on this computer." ## Every request of the window waits for its key · v0.25.1 (2026-11-20)

Fault: `request` in `src/api.ts` sent with whatever key the window held at that moment, and the key is claimed asynchronously (`/api/window/key`, or the shell's `__FORMWEB_WINDOW_KEY`). The window's list of commands (`src/commands/catalogue.ts`, `push`, started from `main.ts` on a timer) could leave before the claim had returned, and the engine refused it with 403. Seen in the window check on a loaded machine: `test_window_loads_throttled_without_shift_errors_or_refusals[/]` failed once in three runs with "requests refused by the guard: /api/commands/catalogue 403". The same code was in 0.24.0. The engine's guard and its open list are unchanged.

`src/api.ts` · the old `loadWindowKey` body is `claimWindowKey`. `loadWindowKey` keeps one promise for the claim (`keyClaim`) and starts a new claim only when the last one finished without a key, which is what a reconnect did before. `windowKeyReady` answers that promise, starting the first claim if nothing has. `request` waits for `windowKeyReady` and then calls `send`, which is the old `request` body. The claim itself asks for its ticket through `send`, not through `request`, or it would wait for itself. `terminalTicket` goes through `request`, so the socket ticket waits too. `requestBlob` and `stream` keep the header as it is at that moment: they are reached only from the shown window, after `connect` has claimed the key.

Run on a scratch clone with this change, `npm ci`, `npm run build` (`tsc --noEmit` and `vite build`, exit 0), then `python -m pytest frontend/tests/window -q -p no:cacheprovider` three times in a row with Playwright 1.63.0: 2 passed each time. Not run: the packaged desktop window, Windows, macOS.

## The desktop shell: one copy, `.fpub`, its own port, allowed commands, and a Preview frame with no sandbox · v0.21.2 (2026-11-15)

Commit ee6f5ce (shell, `src-tauri/`) and the Preview change in `ui/demo-preview.ts`. New dependency: `tauri-plugin-single-instance` 2.5.5 (Apache-2.0 OR MIT), with its 38 new transitive crates in `src-tauri/Cargo.lock`; `LICENSES.md` has the row and the new count (521 registry packages).

The shell's commands never ran. The window shows the page the service serves on 127.0.0.1, and Tauri 2 treats that as a remote origin: a remote page may call an app command only through a capability that lists its address and a permission `allow-<command>` for that command, and `tauri_build::build()` generates no such permission, so every `invoke` of `take_opened_file`, `app_ready`, `set_window_title`, `text_edit` and `render_probe`, `render_thumbnail`, `render_diff` was refused. So opening a `.fweb` from the system, the splash hand-off and text editing, which the 0.21.0 notes listed as "written but never run", could not have worked on any platform. `build.rs` now lists the seven commands through `AppManifest::commands` (Tauri writes `allow-<name>` files into `src-tauri/permissions/autogenerated/`, git-ignored) and `capabilities/default.json` allows them and lists `http://127.0.0.1:(82[4-7][0-9])/*`, ports 8240 to 8279, because the shell may now choose another port.

One copy at a time: `tauri_plugin_single_instance` is the first plugin, as its documentation asks, so a second launch is caught before the rest of the set-up. Its callback hands the second launch's `.fweb` or `.fpub` (`project_arg`, a relative path read from the second launch's own working folder) to `queue_file`, which now exists on every platform (it was macOS only: macOS gets the file as an event, Windows and Linux as an argument), then raises the window only if it is already visible, so a launch during the splash does not show the window early. The page opens the file through the usual path, so an unsaved site gets the usual question. On Linux the plugin keys the hand-over by a name on the D-Bus session bus; with `FORMWEB_HOME` set the name carries an FNV-1a hash of the folder, so a copy with another data folder is its own instance. Without a session bus a second copy still starts (written in `docs/MANUAL.md`). `.fpub` joins `.fweb` in `fileAssociations` of `tauri.conf.json` (`is_project` accepts both, any case).

The port: `probe(port)` asks `/api/health` and answers Free, Ours (a body naming `app: formweb` and the shell's own `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`) or Taken (anything else: another program, another version, a reply that is not JSON). Ours is adopted. Taken makes the shell take the first free port above it, wrapping to the start of 8240 to 8279 (`free_port`, found by binding), start its own service there (`FORMWEB_PORT` is set for the sidecar and for the source run, on Unix and Windows) and open the window on that port (`ENGINE_PORT`). With all 40 ports taken it closes the splash, shows a dialog and exits with 1. `FORMWEB_PORT` inside 8240 to 8279 replaces 8240 as the first choice. Why not adopt a different version: its engine, data format and editor files may differ from the ones the window was built for, and the old rule (any 200) adopted another program's health route. The shell's own service and the other FormWeb may use the same data folder.

`ui/demo-preview.ts`: the Preview frame had `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin ..."`, and Chrome warned on every open that such a frame can remove its own sandbox; the packed page needs both, so the attribute only produced the warning. It is gone. What the sandbox did give, no navigation of the window by the frame, is replaced by `guard()`: while Preview is open the window listens to the Navigation API's `navigate` event and cancels any cancelable navigation except a history traversal (`top.location`, a `_top` link or form, `window.open` to the window). A WebKit without the API gets a `beforeunload` question instead, so the person decides. The guard is removed when Preview closes (the probe `preview_probe2.py` checks that a hash change works again after close). Outside links still open in a new tab or the system browser through the shell's handler.

## The editor holds still at a language switch, the stage header fits, and small editor fixes · v0.21.2 (2026-11-15)

Commit ccc1bbf, editor part.

The export status line: `i18n/index.ts` `Value` gains `{ lang: code }`, and `fill()` writes it with `languageName(code, lang)`: `Intl.DisplayNames` in the interface language (`fallback: 'none'`, so an unknown code is not echoed), else the language menu's own name (`LANG_NAMES`), else the code; `unmade()` was changed to skip these values. `main.ts` `exportSite()` passes `{ lang: locale }`, so the line reads "Static site exported for English." and in German "Statische Website auf Englisch exportiert." (the German word `für` became `auf`). The English source string is unchanged, so only the 23 locale values for "Static site exported for {0}." changed. Why: the 0.21.1 line named the canvas locale code ("für en").

A language switch moves nothing. Why each piece exists, from the measured widths over all 26 languages at 1440 px (Preview 150, Publish 144, toolbar Export and Audit 148, stage readout 176, scrubber Off 84, widest unbreakable label word 106 in nb, widest inspector tab 76 in th and vi): `styles/lang-stable.css` gives the top bar's Publish 144 and Preview 150 (0 at 1280 px and below) and the toolbar's Export and Audit 148 (from a container width of 881) as `min-width`, centred, so English is wider than it needs to be and German is not wider than English; the inspector's four tabs are a fixed grid (two columns in `fw-tabs2`) of 28 px cells whose labels may wrap; the segmented controls (`.fw-seg`) are an equal-column grid by child count; in the two-column field grid each label is one 18 px line with an ellipsis and the Reset link sits over its end on the field's own background, so showing or hiding Reset never wraps a label; the empty scene note stacks. `ui/fit.ts` no longer measures the shown language to choose tabs in one or two rows and fields in one or two columns: tabs decide on room alone, fields on the widest unbreakable word of any language (`ANY_LANGUAGE_FIELD_NEED` 107), and `words()` splits Thai, Japanese, Chinese and Korean text with `Intl.Segmenter` since those scripts have no spaces. `ui/help-fit.ts` (`watchHelp(inspector)`, started in `main.ts`) reserves the height of every inspector help note (`p.micro`, `div.micro`): it lays the note's English source (`englishOf()` in `i18n/index.ts`) out in a hidden probe at the note's width and sets `min-block-size` to 1.5 times the English lines, rounded up, plus one line for notes of four lines or fewer, with a classic 15 px scrollbar assumed so the reserve does not depend on whether a scrollbar shows; no shipped language needed more. English therefore shows blank space under help text. It reruns on resize, on any inspector change and when fonts load. Decision for the owner: that reserved space and the wider buttons are the price of nothing moving.

The stage header: `styles/stage-head.css` and `main.ts` (the readout is two spans, `readout-mode` and `readout-width`, and the page name has its own class) make the header a container (`fw-stage`) with steps at 760, 560 and 400 px of its own width: at 760 the scrubber keeps only its last button and the readout only its width; at 560 the path, the breadcrumbs and the quiet hint go and the scrubber's output and sliders shrink; at 400 the icons go; above 760 the readout never drops below 176 px. Why: at 1440 px a site with scenes cut the readout, and at narrow widths the scrubber was clipped instead of fitting.

Inspector and features: `features/advanced.ts` accepts and documents the state `selected` (the chosen tab or filter chip) next to hover, focus and press, and its example proposes one when the element is a tab or chip trigger of a motion disclosure entry; the help and the parameter hint changed in `i18n/keys.ts` and in the 23 locales. `features/inspector/components.ts`: a poster can belong to one element. An instance with its own poster, or a component placed on more than one element, writes the poster into the instance (`code.instance.set` with `poster` and `posterLight`) instead of the component; the canvas draws the instance's poster first; Clear is enabled only for a poster the element owns, or for the component's poster when only one element uses the component. A component placed once behaves as before. `features/motion/actions.ts`: the search option "Read the from parameter on load" is now "Fill the search from the page address on load" (key and 23 locales), because the page address can now be `?q=`, the field's own name or `?from=`.

Closed by a later change in this release, made by another agent after the entries above were written: the inspector rows the leftovers agent had seen move at a language switch without tracing them fully (wrapping Motion section titles, the spans of the code-component list, and some Content and Page rows), so that no row inside the inspector moves at a switch at 1440 and 1100 px wide. Causes found, measured over 25 languages and the four tabs: tab labels wrapped (Spanish, Thai, Vietnamese); section titles wrapped as name and hint competed for width; checkbox labels and the code-component summary wrapped; the AI Suggest row, the favicon and password rows and the Page tab's Actions buttons reflowed; form-grid columns took their min-content width per language; one Page tab paragraph mixed the Base URL help with a trailing sentence. Fixes, in `lang-stable.css`, `help-fit.ts`, `fit.ts`, `main.ts` and `features/inspector/components.ts`: tab columns reserved at the widest language (62/74/59/56 fr) and never wrapping, with the two-column switch point at the any-language need (254 px); section names on one line, a hint over 16 English characters (`NOTE_BELOW`) on its own full-width row with reserved lines; checkbox labels and summaries reserve lines from the English width (1.7x plus 50 px, then 1.2 for ragged line ends), recomputed when `hidden` or `class` change so the reserve is set before a tab's first paint; the AI row a fixed two-column grid; favicon and password rows an `auto-fill` grid with a 156 px minimum; buttons directly in a section stack one per line with an ellipsis; form-grid columns `minmax(0, 1fr)`; the Base URL sentence its own `p.micro` so help-fit reserves it. Moving rows summed over 50 switches per tab: 1440 from 1076 to 0, 1100 from 894 to 0, 800 from 1140 to 6. Left: at 800 px six Motion tab rows still move and the inspector shows about 0.03 layout shift per switch, cause not located. Cost: blank space under one-line English labels where a long language needs two or three lines. The sculpt section's first screen is described in the library entry below.

## Library: reduced motion in the orbit and sculpt sections, and the product-site starter · v0.21.2 (2026-11-15)

Commit ccc1bbf, `library/` (not covered by a log of its own). `components/orbit-constellation/component.js`: under reduced motion (`calm`) the ring or web was chosen by scroll position alone and the Web chapter was unreachable; clicking a chapter chip now pins that state (`pinned`), scrolling a tall track unpins, the section's "Back to the start" button (`data-or-top`, added to `sections/formvfx-orbit.json`) pins the ring, and the hero also moves up as it fades. `components/glyph-lens/component.js` (the sculpt viewport): under reduced motion the track is no taller than the stage, so the closing block, which the scroll scene fades in over the stage, was never reached as its own screen; `buildTail()` now puts that block in its own section after the track with the picture behind it (`fwc-gl-tail`), hides the scroll-hint widget, and makes "replay" scroll to the section's start; later in the same release the first screen's text was made true under reduced motion: its side note said "Scroll to tune it in" although nothing is tuned by scrolling there, so with reduced motion `note()` shows the new props `asidePointerStill` and `asideTouchStill` (the same sentences without the scroll advice; a visitor-set `asidePointer` or `asideTouch` is still used when no `...Still` text is set), and the stage's spoken label, which also named tuning by scrolling, is replaced by `stageLabelStill` when it still mentions it (restored on destroy); the three props are in the manifest with their `propDocs` and its `fallback` sentence; `rawScroll()` returns 0 for a track no taller than its stage, and the hidden probe element has `transition: none` so it cannot flash. `library/build/components/*.json` were regenerated by `./formweb library build` (generated data). `presets/formai-studio.json`: `ink-faint` `#857e77` to `#8a837c`, 4.33 to 1 to 4.63 to 1 on `float` (AA for small text is 4.5). `sections/formfabric-stories.json` has a visually hidden h1 (`page_title` field, default "FormFabric stories") so the Stories page has a heading, and `starters/product-site.json` gives every page a description of 20 to 200 characters, applied through the backend (backend log) and checked by `library check`.

## The status line after an export or a send names a form that posts to this computer · v0.21.1 (2026-11-15)

Commit 1523c7a, and the export line's own key in the fix(i18n) commit. The 0.21.0 audit row below ended with "Known: the status line after Export static site and Send to FormPublisher still mentions only the 404 base URL warning"; that is closed. `api.ts` `download()` now returns the response `Headers` (it returned nothing), `exportSite()` in `main.ts` reads `X-FormWeb-Local-Forms` through `localFormHeader()` and `sendToFormPublisher()` reads `localForms` from the hand-off reply; `localFormWarning(pages)` writes one `' ' + t(LOCAL_FORM_KEY, [page])` per page, the same key, and so the same 23 translations, as the audit row, which is why no new string was needed for the warning. Why the page names travel and not the sentence: the backend knows only English, and a sentence with a name inside it cannot be matched by the page translator, so each sentence is built here with `t()`. Why a header on Export: the reply is the zip itself, so the names ride in `X-FormWeb-Local-Forms` as percent-encoded JSON (header values are Latin-1 and a page name may be in any script), cut at 20 names to keep it small, and the header is in the CORS `expose_headers` because the dev editor on port 5240 reads it across origins from 8240; `localFormHeader()` treats anything that is not a list of strings as no pages, so a missing or broken header changes nothing. The order on the line is the export or send text, then the 404 base URL warning, then the form warnings; only the base URL warning gets the "Set the base URL" action, because a form has no one field to open (the cure is a script in Motion or Publish). The first half of the Export line, "Static site exported for {locale}.", was a plain template literal, so in German only the warnings were translated; it is now `t('Static site exported for {0}.', [locale])`, key 2565 (the last of `keys.ts`), appended to the 23 locale arrays by hand; `es-MX` and `fr-CA` inherit it from `es` and `fr`. Checked: a site with a posting form, the same site with a scripted reply on the form, and a new site with no form, each through File > Export static site and File > Send to FormPublisher in English and German at 1440 x 900, CPU 4x, with `--no-publisher` (a plain site shows nothing added); `./formweb i18n check` 2565 keys in 23 languages and the "left as English" counts unchanged; `npx tsc --noEmit`.

Beckwith (STScI) and the HUDF Team".
